    with a value that must be evaluable where it is written — one depending on
    a label further down is reported rather than silently resolved to zero —
    and restating a MOS call the assembler already knows is accepted when the
    values agree and reported with both values when they do not, because almost
    every listing opens by writing out the calls it uses.
  - [x] Evidence: 21 catalogue contracts covering internal consistency, unique
    identity, the recorded provenance, every kind of malformed template
    reported at once rather than the first, a duplicate identifier, a missing
    machine profile, an absent variant, ROM set and capability named
    separately, a planned capability refused, the split between what a
    cassette-only machine can and cannot run, a machine with no templates at
    all, and — for each template — parsing through the ordinary project parser
    with nothing arriving already modified, a build target that validates
    against its own machine, a build with no diagnostics that produces the same
    bytes twice, and the project name being taken or defaulted. Seven assembler
    contracts cover the new constants, and four dialog contracts drive the real
    Templates tab: what is offered, the licence line beside each, a template
    opened as a real project with its build target intact, and a machine that
    has none.

### 4.4 Editor shell

Current implemented increment (not a claim that the complete editor tickets
below are finished):

- [x] Editable text surface with line gutter, caret reporting, tabs, indentation,
  keyboard save, file outline, bookmarks, and source download.
- [x] In-file find, previous/next, replace, replace-all, and case matching.
- [x] Bounded current-file/project search and confirmed replace-all with literal,
  case, whole-word, and worker-isolated timed regular-expression modes, grouped
  previews, capture-group replacement, exact file/line/column navigation,
  truncation/error reporting, modified state, and local recovery.
- [x] Explicit command completion for the initial BBC BASIC/6502 vocabulary;
  results insert into the source and are never shown as staged decoration.
- [x] Automatic completion now shares the versioned target-aware provider with
  Ctrl+Space, derives an exact replacement range, incrementally filters after a
  two-character code prefix, and suppresses automatic popups inside BASIC
  strings/REM tails and assembly comments. Stable ranking puts same-file and
  connected project declarations before versioned reference entries; duplicate
  visible declarations are labelled ambiguous rather than silently collapsed.
  Kind, source and version remain textual, and Escape/arrows/Enter/Tab operate
  through the editor's ARIA listbox relationship. Unit and component contracts
  cover context, ranking, ambiguity, automatic invocation, filtering,
  acceptance and suppression. Fuzzy matching, snippets, defines/toolchain
  metadata and unavailable-candidate presentation remain under EDT-209/210.
- [x] BBC BASIC next-line numbering for already-numbered source lines.
- [x] BBC BASIC numbering now accepts configurable start/increment values,
  inserts a safe number from an available gap instead of colliding, and reports
  when no number is available. A whole-program renumber preview validates the
  BBC BASIC II 0–32,767 range, duplicate input numbers and final overflow;
  presents old/new mappings and unresolved targets; updates direct plus
  `ON … GOTO/GOSUB` list references; and applies as one edit with explicit
  undo. One shared scanner preserves strings, DATA and REM during both rewrite
  and jump-target discovery. Unit contracts cover mapping, protected text,
  ambiguity, overflow and gap selection; the production browser contract proves
  preview/apply/undo, three real reference changes, unresolved-target retention,
  overflow refusal and protected-text-aware navigation without browser errors.
  The maintained direct numeric and Atom compact-label forms now have live
  duplicate/missing views and storage-form semantic round-trip evidence.
- [x] Interaction tests cover editing, numbering, completion insertion,
  replace-all, and keyboard save.
- [x] A functional Edit menu now covers plain-text cut/copy/paste, select all,
  complete-line duplicate/delete/move/join, indent/outdent, language-aware BASIC
  and assembly comment toggling, case conversion and trailing-whitespace cleanup.
  Assigned keyboard paths include Ctrl+/ comments, Ctrl+Shift+D/K duplicate/
  delete, Alt+Up/Down move, Tab/Shift+Tab indentation and command Ctrl+Z/Y redo.
  Programmatic edits use a bounded 100-entry per-file history with exact source
  and selection snapshots; intervening typing clears incompatible history.
  Clipboard operations capture the file/selection before awaiting permission,
  cancel against changed source, use plain text only, and expose a labelled
  manual-paste fallback when read permission is denied. Model/component tests
  and a deployed Chromium contract prove exact copy, cut/undo/redo, comment,
  duplicate/move, permission failure and fallback insertion with no browser
  errors. Chromium and Firefox acceptance runs now also prove denied-read
  recovery, literal markup and Unicode insertion, explicit user confirmation,
  automatic command-menu dismissal and zero browser errors. Assistive-
  technology coverage remains part of EDT-205.
- [x] Project files and open editor documents now have separate lifecycle state.
  Explorer selection opens a document; tabs can close individually, close all,
  or close all others without deleting source. Modified content remains in the
  browser project and is stated explicitly when closed. A bounded 20-document
  history supports reopen-last-closed through the File menu and Ctrl+Shift+T,
  including after every editor has closed. Ctrl+W closes the current editor.
  Ctrl+S persists only the current file and leaves unrelated dirty markers
  intact; Ctrl+Shift+S persists all project files. Pure lifecycle and component
  contracts cover active-neighbour selection, close/reopen order, delete-history
  pruning, keyboard routing and per-file save semantics.
- [x] The remaining EDT-009 command set now includes split line, tab-stop-aware
  tab-to-spaces conversion, direct join/case/whitespace shortcuts, C `//` line
  and `/* … */` block comments, and bounded selection/document formatting for
  the registered BBC BASIC and 6502 adapters. Each transformation is one
  command-history entry. Revert requires explicit confirmation, restores the
  last explicit-save content, clears command undo because revert is deliberately
  non-undoable, and can be cancelled without mutation. Project format v5 stores
  saved name/content baselines and an explicit never-saved state separately from
  crash-recovery content, so dirty state and genuine revert survive reload
  without inventing a baseline for a new file; v1–v4 imports migrate forward. Unit,
  component and deployed Chromium contracts prove formatting, splitting,
  recovered dirty state, cancel/confirm revert and zero browser errors.
- [x] The first EDT-216 source-bookmark slice replaces session-only line diamonds
  with named project records persisted in manifest v5. Users can add/remove,
  rename, enable/disable, search and navigate next/previous across files using
  visible controls or Ctrl+Alt+B/PageUp/PageDown. Gutter labels and shapes
  distinguish bookmarks from breakpoints without colour alone. A bounded source
  anchor tracks deterministic line insertion/movement; deleted anchors become
  explicit orphan records with candidate navigation and manual recovery instead
  of silently binding to unrelated code. File deletion cleans owned marks and
  v1–v4 projects migrate safely. Descriptions, persistence-scope choices and
  Project format v14 now adds bounded descriptions and explicit project/private
  persistence scope. Private records remain in browser recovery but portable
  export excludes the complete record by default. A modal export review shows
  exact scope counts, resets to the safe choice whenever opened and requires an
  explicit checkbox before private names, notes, locations or anchors enter the
  download. Imported private records retain their scope. Text badges, action
  labels and gutter states do not rely on colour. Model, component and deployed
  Chromium coverage proves migration, description and scope editing, redacted
  export, explicit inclusion, safe-choice reset and zero browser errors. The
  technical help topic documents the complete workflow, risk boundary and
  recovery with a maintained production screenshot, so EDT-216 is complete.
- [x] Shared initial BBC BASIC/6502 language service provides complete official
  opcode completion, directives, MOS entry points, current-file symbols/lines,
  token definitions/signatures, and resolved or unresolved source links.
- [x] The first Atom BASIC dialect slice replaces the previous empty completion
  catalogue when an Atom is selected. All standard integer-ROM commands,
  functions and connectives listed by *Atomic Theory and Practice* have cited
  structured help, target-aware signatures and completion; BBC-only commands
  remain available only as explicit incompatible help. Atom's semicolon
  statements, apostrophe PRINT newline, integer/address variables, computed or
  lower-case labelled GOTO/GOSUB targets, label-abutted keywords and editor
  `ATOM-BASIC` identity are represented rather than treated as BBC syntax.
  Selecting the floating-point ROM capability conditionally adds its documented
  scientific functions, `%` variables, FDIM/FIF/FINPUT/FPRINT/FPUT/FUNTIL/STR
  and COLOUR vocabulary. Unit contracts cover target filtering, signatures,
  line-label navigation, core/FP types and ROM gating. Because the Atom stores
  numbered BASIC as source text rather than BBC keyword-token records, its
  target-linked build now emits a validated, deterministic ASCII interpreter
  artifact. The ROM-aware adapter enters `NEW`, the source and `RUN` through the
  real Atom keyboard matrix with firmware-safe pacing; a deployed Chromium
  contract verifies output from the genuine interpreter without dropped keys.
  Abbreviation parsing, extension golden fixtures and the static analyser
  dialect remain open, so EDT-203/209/211/212/213 and ANL-309 are not marked
  complete.
- [x] The current-file intelligence slice now derives `PROC`/`FN` completion
  signatures and definitions from real `DEF` declarations, follows active BASIC
  arguments while ignoring nested/string commas, and presents non-focus-stealing
  parameter help automatically or via Ctrl+Shift+Space. F12 and Ctrl/Command-
  click navigate assembly labels, BASIC line numbers and BASIC routines to exact
  source ranges; routine calls/declarations are also exposed in jump/outline
  views. A reusable document-version coordinator aborts old requests and rejects
  late results; completion uses it now. Unit contracts and a production browser
  flow prove SOUND argument 4/4, `PROCdraw` definition navigation and dynamic
  completion without browser errors. Cross-file/SDK navigation, overload forms,
  full type metadata and migration of every future async provider remain open
  under EDT-208–EDT-215 rather than being implied by this increment.
- [x] The next EDT-213 signature slice resolves the innermost still-open BASIC
  invocation, so declared nested `FN`/`PROC` calls receive their own active
  parameter and the enclosing command resumes after the nested call closes.
  Tokens inside strings and REM commentary cannot take over the active call.
  Structured alternative forms for `PRINT`, `VDU` and `CALL` expose optional
  and repeated parameters in one live, screen-reader-labelled status region;
  automatic and Ctrl+Shift+Space invocation leave editor focus untouched.
  Keyboard form selection, broader built-in functions and C/ARM overload
  providers remain open, so EDT-213 is intentionally not marked complete.
- [x] A project-language index now versions the exact content/name/language of
  every source file and invalidates completion, caret help, signature and
  definition requests when either the active document or another project file
  changes. Requests superseded on the same provider channel are aborted as
  well. Displayed completion/help metadata carries a non-source-revealing
  project version and its built-in/project origin.
- [x] 6502 cross-file intelligence follows the assembler's actual `INCLUDE`
  graph as an undirected connected build scope. Automatic and Ctrl+Space
  completion includes symbols only from that scope, offers compatible project
  filenames while typing `INCLUDE`, filters opcodes for 6502 versus 65C02/65C12,
  uses exact replacement ranges, and accepts Enter or Tab. F12, Ctrl/Command-
  click and the jump-target view open closed included files at exact symbol
  ranges. Duplicate connected declarations present an accessible choice list;
  symbols elsewhere in the project are reported as out of graph rather than
  guessed. BASIC routines and line numbers intentionally remain file-local
  because the current BASIC build adapter does not link source modules.
- [x] Optional type hints expose only authoritative selected-dialect types: BBC
  BASIC II `%` signed 32-bit integers, `$` variable-length strings and
  unsuffixed 5-byte reals; or Atom BASIC single-letter/doubled-array signed
  32-bit integers/address holders plus five-byte `%` variables only when the
  floating-point extension ROM is selected. They are sourced from assignments
  and declared routine parameters where the dialect supports them, while
  ignoring strings and REM text. 6502/ARM assembly explicitly reports untyped
  source and receives no invented types. This is a navigable side-panel slice,
  not yet the configurable editor-overlay inlay system required to finish
  EDT-211.
- [x] The first EDT-215 navigation slice adds version-guarded Shift+F12 Find
  References for 6502/BASIC symbols, a non-modal accessible peek list with
  declaration/reference source excerpts, exact cross-file selection, and a
  bounded 100-location Back/Forward stack. Navigation reopens closed source
  editors and restores file, line, column, selection length and scroll offset;
  a new jump clears the forward branch. Implementation/type-definition, call
  hierarchy, split restoration and broader C/ARM reference providers remain
  open, so EDT-215 itself is intentionally not marked complete.
- [x] The first rich EDT-212 reference slice replaces generic 6502-family
  opcode descriptions with structured, decoder-derived syntax/addressing forms,
  examples, results, flags, memory/register/stack effects, fixed or variable
  cycle ranges and selected-CPU compatibility. Hover, keyboard focus and caret
  help render this metadata in a dismissible inline region with textual
  supported/incompatible states, reference-pack version and a direct WDC
  W65C02S datasheet citation. Known CMOS-only instructions remain excluded from
  NMOS completion but explicit help reports their incompatibility. The same
  structured model now covers the initial BBC BASIC command set and the complete
  known MOS entry-point table: documented interfaces include parameter/register
  ranges, results, effects, examples, related calls and direct Acorn guide
  citations. Language requests carry the selected CPU, machine, ROM identity,
  ROM-readiness and capabilities; incompatible BASIC/MOS entries are filtered
  from completion while explicit lookup explains the mismatch, and open help is
  re-resolved on target changes. Electron ENVELOPE/MODE limits and missing local
  ROM execution prerequisites are stated rather than hidden. Hardware registers,
  ARM, C, SWIs, additional BASIC vocabulary, richer local reference packs and
  exhaustive semantic review remain open; EDT-212 is intentionally not complete.
- [x] Initial accessible command palette with searchable typed commands,
  category/shortcut metadata, keyboard navigation, contextual disabled reasons,
  and live project/file/editor/build/run/debug/runtime/panel/workspace actions.
  Global palette, project-search, go-to-line, build, run and debug shortcuts are
  functional; remapping and exhaustive EDT-008 coverage remain open.

- [x] EDT-200 Implement tabs, splits, diff, editor preferences, file encodings,
  line endings, large-file behavior, and semantic dirty/read-only/generated states.
  - [x] A validated browser-local preference schema now controls source font
    size, line height, 2/4/8-column tab display and wrapping across every open
    file. Bounds keep gutter and textarea rows aligned, malformed or denied
    storage falls back to documented defaults, and Reset restores the complete
    schema. Theme colours remain exclusively in the overrideable theme layer.
    A two-pane source split now gives each pane independent file, caret,
    selection, scroll, language UI and command state over shared project source.
    Visible controls and Ctrl+Backslash open or close it, active-pane state is
    exposed in text and with a theme-token focus edge, and the responsive layout
    stacks panes below 900 pixels. Project format v15 now persists current and
    saved UTF-8, UTF-8 BOM or Windows-1252 encoding plus LF, CRLF or CR line
    endings. Byte-aware import validates UTF-8, falls back to Windows-1252,
    detects line endings and normalizes editor text; byte-aware download
    reconstructs the selected convention and refuses unrepresentable legacy
    characters. Format changes participate in dirty, save and revert state.
    Source above 256 KiB announces large mode and pauses eager completion,
    outline, type-hint and per-line gutter work while core editing remains.
    Hard 1 MiB per-file and 8 MiB project source bounds cover imports, portable
    manifests and edits. Model, project, component and deployed browser coverage
    proves Windows-1252 CR import, normalized source, UTF-8 BOM CRLF download,
    status, large-mode fallback and zero browser errors. Maintained technical
    help includes the operational limits and a production screenshot. A saved
    baseline versus working-copy comparison now reports textual added and
    removed states, keeps both physical line columns, navigates working rows,
    and switches to an explicitly labelled bounded algorithm for large line
    matrices. Unit, component and real-browser evidence cover its model,
    operation and illustrated technical help. Remaining semantic
    read-only/generated state work completes the slice. Project format v16
    persists authored/imported/generated provenance, editable/read-only access
    and bounded generator text. Generated records are forced read-only during
    import. Explorer, tab, breadcrumb, policy banner and inspector expose the
    state as text, while editor and project update boundaries block mutation
    and retain inspection, navigation, comparison, copy and download. Model,
    component and real-browser tests plus maintained illustrated help provide
    the acceptance evidence.
- [x] EDT-201 Implement accessible file/project find and replace with preview.
  - [x] Evidence: 6 project-search contracts covering the query, match and
   character bounds and the reporting of a search that stopped early rather
   than presenting a partial result as complete, with the replace preview
   driven through the real editor by the 63 source-workspace contracts.
- [x] EDT-202 Implement outline/function/symbol selectors, go-to line/address,
  command palette, and shortcut system.
  - [x] The first selector slice adds a keyboard-operable project symbol search
    backed by the parsed index shared with completion, definition, references
    and rename. Independent search terms match names, signatures, kinds and
    filenames; Enter opens the first result at its exact file, line, column and
    declaration length, Escape clears the query, and the bounded 200-result
    view reports its limit. Assembly labels, BASIC lines, Atom labels, BBC
    BASIC procedures and functions are authoritative today. Constants, macros,
    C declarations, a unified go-to address surface and shortcut remapping keep
    EDT-202 open.
  - [x] Ctrl+G and the command palette now open an accessible line-or-symbol
    dialog instead of a browser prompt. It starts on the current physical line,
    accepts optional colon line notation, filters the same parsed project index
    with independent terms, supports wrapping Up/Down selection, Enter, Escape
    and pointer activation, and navigates exact declaration ranges. Numeric
    source lines are explicitly distinguished from BASIC program lines and
    machine addresses. Source-to-address navigation still requires current
    build debug metadata.
  - [x] Shortcut remapping is delivered under UX-106: every dispatched
    workbench and editor chord is declared in one table, listed in Settings ›
    Keyboard shortcuts, and can be changed, unbound or reset with conflict and
    host-ownership reporting.
  - [x] The last piece was the go-to surface telling the truth about what it
    could not find. An empty result list reads as "there is no such thing",
    which is a different statement from "this build carries no address map" —
    and only one of them is usually true. Every way of coming up empty now says
    which: a build with no source-to-address map says so and says how to get
    one, an address below everything the build maps names the lowest mapped
    address, something that is not hexadecimal is told so with the three
    notations that work, a physical line past the end of the file names the
    file's length, and a search that matched nothing repeats the terms it
    tried. The exact and nearest-preceding address paths both navigate.
  - [x] Constants, macros and C declarations were reachable through the same
    surface once the index learned them, and are now pinned as such: assembly
    constants and macros, C object-like and function-like defines, typedefs and
    tagged types, functions and file-scope variables, BBC BASIC procedures and
    numbered lines are each findable by name, and by kind, so every constant in
    a project can be listed at once.
  - [x] Evidence: 9 further dialog contracts covering all five empty-result
    explanations, both address paths, every declaration kind the index parses
    reached through the real control, and search by kind.
- [x] EDT-203 Define language adapter API and implement syntax/outline/diagnostic
  baseline for the first 6502 dialect and project manifests.
  - [x] Language behaviour was spread across the product: the outline was a
    private helper inside the editor component, classification was implicit in
    the editor's markup, and the per-language branches were `if (file.language
    === ...)` chains in several files at once. That works until a dialect is
    added, at which point it has to be added everywhere and is inevitably added
    in some places and not others. An adapter is now the one declaration of
    what a language offers — classification, outline and single-file
    diagnostics — with a registry, a resolver and a validator run by a
    contract.
  - [x] The boundary is deliberate and is the reason the API is worth having.
    An adapter sees one file, so it reports only what one file can support: a
    label declared twice in this file is a fact, an unresolved symbol is not,
    because an included file the adapter cannot see may declare it. Calling
    that an error would be wrong for every project that uses INCLUDE.
    Whole-project questions stay with the project language service, which has
    the graph.
  - [x] A language with no adapter gets nothing rather than a stub. Undefined
    is a real answer: an adapter that returned an empty outline and no
    diagnostics would look like a working one and be believed. The editor keeps
    its older flat scan for those languages, which is honestly less than an
    adapter rather than a panel pretending otherwise.
  - [x] The 6502 adapter is the first dialect. Classification separates
    mnemonics, directives, labels, Acorn, dollar, C and binary numbers, strings
    and comments, and does not mistake a semicolon inside a string for a
    comment. The outline nests: an include is a statement about the file and
    stays at the top, while a constant declared inside a label's body belongs
    to it, with the constant's value and a macro's parameters carried as the
    detail. Diagnostics report a label declared twice in this file naming the
    first, a constant restated at a different value naming both, and every
    unbalanced macro — never closed, opened inside another, closed without
    being opened.
  - [x] Project manifests get an adapter too. The asset, tile-map, palette,
    font, song, test-plan, disk-set, settings, bundle, annotation and project
    documents this product defines are JSON that a person edits by hand often
    enough to deserve an outline and a straight answer when a brace is missing,
    rather than being treated as plain text. A schema this build knows passes
    silently; one it does not know is reported as uncheckable rather than as
    wrong. Locating a JSON failure took three message shapes — a line and
    column, a byte position, and an excerpt of the surrounding text — and where
    none of them yields a position the diagnostic says the parser did not
    report where, rather than pointing at line one as though it meant it.
  - [x] The editor's outline panel now comes from the registry, indented by
    nesting depth, with a document-issues section that navigates to the exact
    position.
  - [x] Evidence: 35 adapter contracts covering the registry and its validator,
    every classification case, the nesting rules including a macro that closes
    at ENDMACRO, exact line and column, each diagnostic, the silence about
    symbols an adapter cannot see, every manifest state, and a property that no
    reported JSON position ever falls outside the document; plus 5 workspace
    contracts driving the real panel — nesting and detail on screen, a
    duplicate label navigated to, silence for an included symbol, a manifest
    outlined and its broken JSON reported, and the flat fallback for a language
    with no adapter.
- [x] EDT-204 Add number converter and target/toolchain-aware address formatting.
  The source sidebar accepts decimal, Acorn and dollar hexadecimal, C
  hexadecimal, binary and octal notation at explicit 8, 16 or 32-bit widths.
  It reports unsigned and two's-complement signed values, fixed-width binary,
  octal, printable low-byte text, and exact little/big-endian byte order. The
  preferred literal follows ca65, Acorn-style, C or ARM source context. Address
  validation separately enforces the 16-bit 6502-family range or the
  word-aligned ARM 26-bit range. Parser, bounds, endian and address contracts
  plus accessible component interaction are automated.
- [x] EDT-205 Test keyboard, screen reader semantics, 200% zoom, reflow, large
  files, international input, and undo isolation across editor/asset surfaces.
  - [x] Every one of these is invisible in ordinary use, which is why they are
    contracts rather than something someone checks by hand. A surface with no
    accessible name looks identical to one that has a name; an undo that
    reaches into another document looks like nothing at all until someone
    loses work; a composition event only appears when text is entered through
    an input method rather than typed a character at a time.
  - [x] Fifteen editor contracts: the editing surface named by the file it
    edits rather than by "Edit", every region named, no interactive control
    without an accessible name, a single-file problem reported as a word and
    not by colour; the editor, the outline and the symbol search all reachable
    and focusable from the keyboard, Escape clearing the search, and parameter
    help that does not take focus — help that steals focus makes an editor
    unusable with a screen reader, because every keystroke then lands somewhere
    else; a composition kept rather than dropped, characters outside the basic
    plane kept whole rather than cut in half into a replacement character,
    non-Latin lines counted correctly; large-source mode saying what it paused
    and what remains, naming the size, and keeping the file editable; and undo
    isolated per document, with one editor command undone as one step.
  - [x] Reflow and zoom are checked in a real browser and are now part of the
    release gate rather than a one-off, because a layout regression is
    invisible until someone is using a small window and by then it has
    shipped. Five sizes, down to the 320 CSS pixels WCAG 1.4.10 names — which
    is also what 400% zoom on a 1280-pixel display produces. The measurement
    uses the browser's own visibility test, so a closed disclosure is not
    counted as content that has to fit, and it treats a control inside a
    horizontally scrollable strip as reachable, because scrolling a tab rail is
    what a tab rail is for.
  - [x] It found three real defects, all of them present at sizes people
    actually use. The editor toolbar ran off the right of the window at the
    reference size of 1440 pixels, with seven controls unreachable, because it
    could neither wrap nor scroll; a rule intended to help at narrow widths was
    in fact forcing it wider than the viewport. The emulator toolbar did the
    same below 640 pixels. Both now wrap: they hold popup menus whose panels a
    scroll container would clip, so wrapping is the only correct answer. With
    those fixed the workbench reflows cleanly at every size down to 320 pixels
    — no horizontal scrolling, nothing out of reach, and no console errors.
- [x] EDT-206 Implement the complete editing command set in EDT-009, including
  cut/copy/paste, line and selection operations, comments, indentation,
  formatting, save-all, reopen-closed, keyboard bindings, and predictable undo.
  - [x] Evidence: 6 editor-operation contracts for the transformations, 3
   document-lifecycle contracts for close, reopen and save-all, 3 clipboard
   contracts, 3 preference contracts, and 6 key-binding dispatch contracts
   proving every chord resolves from the one binding table. Undo
   predictability is covered by the source-workspace contracts, including one
   proving a command is undone as a single step rather than character by
   character.
- [x] EDT-207 Implement clipboard permission/error fallbacks, plain-text paste,
  generated/stale-data safeguards, and cross-browser clipboard tests (EDT-010).
  - [x] A typed plain-text clipboard adapter now preserves literal Unicode and
    markup-looking content, classifies unavailable, denied, aborted, oversized
    and generic failures, and bounds reads and writes to 8 Mi characters. The
    editor falls back to a labelled manual paste surface with the exact failure
    reason and explicit Insert or Cancel. Direct and fallback paste plus delayed
    cut recheck file ID, complete source snapshot and captured selection before
    mutation. Source copy is limited to the visible selection or active line;
    generated build documents remain in their separate read-only viewer. Unit
    and component tests cover API success, absence, permission denial, abort,
    bounds, Unicode, literal markup and delayed stale results. Visible Chromium
    and Firefox acceptance runs prove the denied-permission workflow, explicit
    literal insertion, Unicode and markup preservation, automatic menu
    dismissal and zero page or console errors. The maintained in-app help image
    records the same permission-recovery operation.
- [x] EDT-208 Implement versioned, cancellable language-service requests so late
  completion, hover, signature, navigation, and code-action results cannot act
  on newer document/build states (EDT-013, EDT-028).
  The shared session now binds every asynchronous provider request to document
  ID, monotonic document version, complete project-index revision, target and
  build revision, provider channel and request ID. Opening a changed document,
  target or build aborts all outstanding providers; a newer same-channel
  request aborts its predecessor; explicit channel/all cancellation and
  disposal propagate AbortSignal. Every late result is checked again after its
  provider settles. Completion retains the exact response identity and rejects
  activation after supersession. Caret help, explicit signatures, definition
  and references use the same contract. Multi-file rename previews expire on
  any indexed source/build/target revision and are recomputed immediately
  before mutation. Unit and component contracts cover document, project,
  channel, explicit cancellation, build revision, displayed completion and
  stale code-action behavior.
- [ ] EDT-209 Implement context-aware completion for commands, opcodes,
  directives, keywords, labels, BASIC line targets, types, functions, variables,
  registers, hardware/OS symbols, SWIs, include paths, defines, and snippets,
  filtered by dialect, CPU, machine, ROM, toolchain, target, and scope.
  - [x] The first syntax-position slice distinguishes assembly mnemonic, branch
    target and general operand slots; restricts 6502 branch and call operands to
    connected labels, active target defines and compatible MOS entries; offers
    ARM R0 to R15 plus SP, LR and PC only in operand positions; and restricts
    BBC and Atom control-flow operands to declared numeric or label targets.
    Assembly INCLUDE and INCLUDEASSET complete compatible project inputs. C
    include operands complete project headers and the active cc65 BBC SDK with
    matching closing delimiters. Exact build defines and include paths now
    participate in target revision invalidation. Model and component tests prove
    position filtering, numeric replacement ranges, CPU filtering, connected
    scope, SDK/project headers and accessible candidate presentation. Local
    variables, C members, macros, hardware registers, SWIs, generated symbols
    and snippets keep EDT-209 open.
  - [x] The declaration and target-reference slice indexes assembly constants
    and macros, typed BBC BASIC variables, C variables and functions, object and
    function macros, and quoted C header relationships. Syntax filtering keeps
    macros in mnemonic positions, constants and hardware addresses in operands,
    and unreachable headers out. Machine-filtered BBC, Master, Electron, Atom
    and early Archimedes hardware entries insert the numeric dialect required by
    BeebAsm, ca65, BBC BASIC, C or GNU as and carry technical side-effect warnings
    and maintained manual citations. A dedicated ARM SWI operand slot supplies
    the first RISC OS kernel/FileSwitch set with number, register contract,
    result, ROM availability, PRM citations and buildable GNU as insertion.
    C function parameters and preceding locals are now limited by function,
    declaration order and nested brace lifetime and do not leak into another
    routine or a closed block. Dot and pointer-arrow completion resolves ordinary
    named and typedef structure members across quoted connected headers without
    mixing unrelated receiver types. Exact current assembler/linker symbols and
    their resolved addresses participate in language revisioning, merge with a
    matching source declaration without false ambiguity, and disappear as soon
    as provenance becomes stale. The first reviewed
    snippet library inserts buildable BASIC statement, 6502 BBC MOS, ARM RISC OS
    and cc65 C patterns, filters them by machine and toolchain, and replaces full
    underscore identifiers as one undoable editor operation.
  - [x] Complex C declarators are now read by a declarator parser rather than by
    a `type name` pattern. The pattern covered the simple half of the language
    and quietly missed the half Acorn C is written in: `char *names[8]` is an
    array of pointers and was reported as a pointer, `void (*handler)(int)` is a
    function pointer and was reported as a variable of type `void`, `int a, *b,
    c[3]` is three different types flattened into one, and
    `unsigned char screen[8][8]` lost a dimension. None of that failed loudly —
    a missed declaration simply makes the completion list short, which reads as
    "this file has no such symbol" and is wrong. The parser reads the grammar
    the way C reads it, from the innermost identifier outwards, and returns
    nothing for a declaration it cannot read rather than guessing, so nothing
    downstream can present a type it did not produce. For a function it reports
    the return type, because that is the question a reader is asking, with the
    parameters separate and the declaration carried as written.
  - [x] Conditional macros are reported against what the build target actually
    defines. Every `#define` in a file used to be offered as though it were
    unconditional, which is wrong in the way that matters most: it suggests a
    symbol that will not exist when the code is built, and the failure appears
    at compile time with no connection back to the suggestion that caused it. A
    declaration in a branch this build does not take is now offered as
    unavailable with the condition named, rather than hidden — which would make
    it unfindable. The third state is the important one: a condition like
    `#if VERSION > 2` needs a value and an expression evaluator, so it is
    reported as unsettled rather than guessed, because guessing would be a
    confidence this product has not earned. A decided operand still settles the
    whole expression where the logic allows it.
  - [x] Evidence: 17 declarator contracts covering every construct above, each
    declarator's own type in a multiple declaration, struct, union, enum and
    typedef names, initialisers dropped, pointer-to-pointer, a name offset that
    always lands on the name, and every shape it refuses to read; 16
    preprocessor contracts covering the guard structure, `#ifdef`, `#ifndef`,
    `#else`, `#elif`, nesting, the `#if 0` idiom, the forms that can and cannot
    be decided, short-circuiting, and the three sentences a person is shown;
    and 7 index contracts proving all of it reaches completion through the real
    service.
  - [ ] Complete hardware and module SWI catalogues, debug type records and
    linked snippet fields keep EDT-209 open. The two catalogues are the ones
    that cannot be written from here: they are reference data — RISC OS SWI
    numbers and register contracts, hardware register maps — and writing them
    from memory would put invented technical detail in front of users. They
    need a cited source in the repository first, the way the MOS entry-point
    table and the W65C02S instruction data already have one.
- [x] EDT-210 Implement accessible completion interaction: explicit/automatic
  trigger, filtering/ranking, candidate kind and source, signature/summary,
  replacement range, commit characters, keyboard acceptance, dismissal,
  ambiguity, incompatibility, and stale-result states (EDT-011–EDT-013).
  - [x] Explicit completion now retains maintained but target-incompatible
    matches as non-inserting UNAVAILABLE options with the exact compatibility
    warning, while automatic completion continues to exclude them. Kind and
    project/reference glyphs have adjacent text equivalents. Options expose
    selected, disabled, position and set-size semantics; pointer, Enter and Tab
    activation of an unavailable item only announces its reason. Existing
    version-bound acceptance rejects stale source, project, target and build
    results. Component and browser tests prove source remains unchanged.
  - [x] Completion now finds a candidate by the characters in it rather than
    only the ones it starts with: typing every character of `draw_sprite` to
    reach it is not how anyone works, and was the only way. A match is scored
    by where the characters land — the start of a token, or a boundary after an
    underscore, a dot or a capital, is worth far more than the middle of a word
    — with a run kept together, every skipped character costing, a shorter
    candidate preferred, and the typed case rewarded. Characters not present in
    order at all mean the candidate is not offered, rather than offered last.
  - [x] Scattered matches are a separate, lower tier and are never mixed into
    the first. Someone who typed the start of a name expects that name, and a
    cleverer ranking that put something else above it would be worse than no
    ranking at all. They also stay out until two characters have been typed:
    with one, every token containing that letter would qualify, which is a list
    nobody can read. A candidate found that way marks the characters that
    matched and says it matched out of order, so it explains its own presence
    rather than looking like an unrelated suggestion.
  - [x] Commit punctuation is deliberately sparse, because a commit character
    that fires when someone meant to type the character silently rewrites what
    they wrote — which is worse than not having one. So `(` accepts a callable,
    and in assembly `,` and `)` accept a symbol, because an operand ends with
    one or the other and with nothing else. Nothing commits on a letter, digit,
    space, full stop or hyphen, since all of them occur inside real tokens, and
    a commit character types normally whenever no candidate is selected. One
    function decides the set for every candidate, applied on the way out of the
    completion service so a producer cannot declare a set that disagrees with
    it.
  - [x] Marking the matched characters individually would have made a screen
    reader announce a token letter by letter, because a name computed from an
    option's contents inserts a separator between element children. Each option
    now carries an explicit accessible name built from the same parts as its
    visible text, so the two cannot diverge.
  - [x] Evidence: 14 further model contracts covering the match scoring,
    boundaries, camel case, tightness, length, case, the two-character
    threshold, tier ordering, reported match positions, and every commit-
    character rule including the characters that must never commit; plus 4
    editor contracts driving the real completion list — a symbol accepted on a
    comma with the comma typed, the same key typing normally with nothing
    open, a scattered match shown with its marked characters and its
    explanation, and a prefix match ranked above one that merely contains the
    letters.
- [ ] EDT-211 Implement optional type/inlay hints for languages with authoritative
  type data, including size/signedness/address-space/parameters/returns and honest
  unavailable or untyped states (EDT-014).

  - [x] The compiler debug records this item was waiting for are now read, and
    the answer about types is a boundary rather than a feature. ld65 has been
    writing a debug file on every full-metadata C build and it was retained as
    a document nothing opened; it is now parsed. What the pinned toolchain
    records about types is nothing: cc65 2.19-1 emits exactly one type record,
    `id=0,val="00"`, and points every C symbol at it. So this build says that
    plainly instead of decoding a type it would have had to infer from
    somewhere else. What the file does carry about a C symbol is exact and is
    now reported — its storage class, its frame offset, and the address of the
    assembler symbol behind a static or extern — alongside the addresses each
    line of C actually produced and where each segment was linked. The reader
    checks its own completeness against the record counts the file states about
    itself and refuses a file it could only partly read, because a debug file
    read in part is worse than one not read at all: everything built on it
    would be confidently incomplete. The one stated count this toolchain does
    not mean as a record count is reported rather than enforced, and said so.
  - [x] Evidence: 11 parser contracts and 2 document contracts, checked against
    a real ld65 debug file kept exactly as the pinned toolchain produced it from
    `src/build/fixtures/cc65-debug-info.c` — not one written to suit the parser.
    The completeness check is proved by deleting records from that real file and
    watching the file itself notice.

### Phase 2 exit gate

- [x] LOCAL-GATE A user can create/import/edit/save/recover/export a local,
  validated machine-aware project entirely by keyboard; exercise completion,
  hover documentation, type/signature hints, click and keyboard target jumps,
  bookmarks, BASIC numbering/renumbering, find/replace, clipboard and core edit
  commands; no compiler/emulator is falsely implied and the container/quality
  foundation passes from a fresh clone.
  - [x] Aggregate model, component and deployed-browser evidence covers every
    listed operation, including pointer and keyboard target opening, exact
    clipboard fallback behavior and capability-aware unavailable states. A
    clean-storage Playwright journey on port 8090 used keyboard operation to
    select linked Atom and BBC B targets, complete PRINT, inspect a SOUND
    signature, open a PROC declaration, create a bookmark, replace two routine
    references, reverse a language-aware comment edit, append automatically
    numbered line 120, save and recover after reload, then export, clear and
    re-import the portable project. It reported no console, page, HTTP or
    workflow errors. The complete frontend suite passes 106 files and 531
    tests, technical-help verification passes 42 maintained screenshot
    references and 16 control contracts, the clean Docker build succeeds, and
    both port-8090 services report healthy.

## 5. Phase 3 — build, diagnostics, artifacts, and media

### 5.1 Adapter runtime and sandbox

- [ ] BLD-300 Implement toolchain manifest/registry and common adapter lifecycle
  (BLD-001, API-007).
  - [x] Browser-local adapters and the readiness-gated `cc65.ca65-ld65` and
    `stardot.beebasm` server adapters share build target, result, provenance and
    artifact contracts without conflating their source dialects.
- [ ] BLD-301 Implement disposable non-root sandbox, immutable toolchains,
  network denial, resource/output/process limits, cancellation, and cleanup.
  - [x] The local ca65/BeebAsm worker implements this boundary in a dedicated Symfony
    PHP-FPM container; public multi-tenant per-job container scheduling remains
    part of BLD-302 rather than being implied by this local-mode completion.
- [ ] BLD-302 Add per-tenant job fairness, idempotency, state transitions,
  progress, retry rules, terminal retention, and audit trail (API-003).
- [ ] BLD-303 Implement normalized build result/provenance, logs, diagnostics,
  symbols, source map, memory/size map, and artifacts (BLD-009).
  - [x] ca65/ld65 and BeebAsm successes and diagnostic-only failures use the common result
    envelope; successful binaries flow unchanged into run, test and debugger
    consumers and expose listing/map/labels/debug/linker documents read-only.
- [ ] BLD-304 Implement content-addressed cache with integrity, tenant separation,
  declared inputs, metrics, invalidation tests, and bypass control.
- [ ] BLD-305 Test malicious filenames/options/sources, runaway tools, fork/
  output bombs, network attempts, cancellation races, and cleanup failures.
  - [x] Path traversal, absolute/dynamic includes, `INCBIN`, invalid filenames,
    request/file/unit/define/address bounds, timeout/output terminals, regular-
    file enforcement and job cleanup have automated coverage. Fork/memory bombs,
    forced cleanup failure and cancellation-race stress remain open.

### 5.2 Build experience

Current implemented increment (the complete sandbox/toolchain tickets remain
open):

- [x] The browser-local portion of BLD-300 now has one typed adapter registry
  for BBC BASIC II, Atom BASIC, NMOS 6502 and Acorn 65C12. Detection returns
  pinned manifests exactly once, each adapter declares supported build profiles,

  workers, build-all, Run, Debug and Test. NMOS/65C12 share implementation while
  retaining distinct processor manifests. Capability rejection and registry
  uniqueness have dedicated contracts; the complete 50-file/218-test suite and
  production build pass, and a deployed two-worker build-all contract reports
  zero browser errors. The ca65 and BeebAsm local native slices now share this
  lifecycle too; public multi-tenant scheduling and isolation remain open under
  BLD-300–BLD-302 rather than being implied by the local worker.

  command-independent metadata for every completed adapter invocation: pinned

  explicit cache-bypass status, declared input fingerprints and SHA-256 digests,
  SHA-256 output and canonical pinned-toolchain-manifest digests, artifact records,

  bounded lifecycle logs. Worker structured-clone/build-all consumption and
  deterministic artifact/provenance equality are contract tested. The envelope
  is retained per target and exposed/exportable as a generated read-only

  use the same typed envelope with normalized diagnostics and an empty artifact
  collection; workers/build-all preserve it and the UI explicitly says no

  A deployed Chromium contract proves invalid-output failure, zero fabricated

  zero browser errors. Cache integration remains open under BLD-304.
- [x] The browser-session portion of BLD-304 now uses a bounded content-addressed
  cache (24 entries/8 MiB including declared source bytes). Its SHA-256 key
  covers target/dependency declarations, machine profile, explicit dependency
  inputs and the recursively resolved INCLUDE/source graph while ignoring
  unrelated project files. Hits re-verify output bytes, immutable source bytes,

### 5.3 Media and artifact workbench

Current implemented increment:

- [x] Live machine Media workspace validates a bounded local DFS/ADFS filename
  and size, selects drive 0/1, transfers bytes to the active jsbeeb FDC, and
  displays only the emulator's real mount acknowledgement. Browser coverage
  mounts a 200 KiB SSD image into drive 1.
- [x] Bounded UEF and tapefile cassette input is signature-validated before the
  emulator parser, attached to the real BBC/Electron ACIA or Atom PPIA, and
  acknowledged independently of concurrently mounted disk media. Deterministic
  contracts cover signatures, empty/unknown/oversized rejection, BBC ACIA and
  Atom PPIA attachment. The UI discloses that current state downloads do not
  preserve cassette stream position; recording remains future work.
- [x] Read-only DFS SSD catalogue inspection decodes title, cycle, boot option,
  declared/physical sector geometry, file/directory names, lock flags, 18-bit
  load/execute/length fields and start sectors before mounting. It reports
  invalid structure, geometry mismatches, duplicate names and out-of-bounds
  extents without modifying the source image. Unit and live-browser fixtures
  prove metadata decoding while DSD/ADFS are explicitly not claimed by this
  first reader.
- [x] A deterministic DFS writer packages one or more logical files in an
  80-track, single-sided 200 KiB SSD. A warning-free SSD can be opened into an
  editable logical project; disk title, cycle, boot option, catalogue order,
  file/directory name, lock state and 18-bit load/execute addresses can be
  changed, files removed, and the immutable current build added. Draft changes
  invalidate the previous download and mount candidate. Before either path is
  re-enabled, the independent reader reparses geometry/catalogue fields and
  byte-compares every extent. Contracts cover repeat identity, duplicate/invalid
  metadata, address bounds, capacity and import/edit/rewrite. A headed browser
  acceptance imported a two-file SSD, preserved cycle 42 and a locked 300-byte
  payload, removed `$.!BOOT`, renamed `$.GAME` to `$.PLAY`, changed title/boot,
  rebuilt/downloaded it, and independently reparsed it with zero warnings.
- [x] Exact 400 KiB, 80-track DFS DSD images are treated as two physical sides,
  not as an oversized SSD. The adapter deinterleaves conventional 10-sector
  tracks, opens each side through the independent SSD parser, supports empty or
  populated sides, edits either catalogue, then reinterleaves and byte-compares
  the final image. The editor can add bounded arbitrary host files as well as
  immutable build artifacts. A headed port-8090 acceptance preserved side 0's
  300-byte `$.GAME`, side 1's locked `$.!BOOT`, added an exact 13-byte
  `$.HOSTFIL`, changed one side title, exported 409,600 bytes, and independently
  reparsed both sides with their original cycle/boot metadata and no warnings.

- [x] MED-300 Define logical-file and media adapter contracts plus independent
  reader/writer validation (MED-001, MED-006).
  - [x] Typed multi-file DFS logical project/build-artifact request/result,
    warning-free SSD import/edit, and writer→independent-reader byte validation
    are implemented.
  - [x] DFS unknown-metadata preservation. Opening an image now captures the
    catalogue bytes this adapter does not model — the two bits of the boot-option
    byte Acorn DFS leaves unused and third-party filing systems do not, and
    whatever follows the last catalogue entry in each catalogue sector — and
    rewriting lays them down before the entries that are modelled, so both
    catalogue sectors come back byte for byte when nothing has changed. Where a
    larger catalogue has to claim some of those bytes, the count is returned and
    reported in the interface rather than left to be discovered by comparing
    images.
  - [x] The declared disc geometry is carried rather than assumed. A 40-track
    catalogue is written back as 400 sectors instead of being silently reissued
    as an 800-sector claim, a set that does not fit names both the sectors it
    needs and the sectors the disc declares, and a geometry a DFS catalogue
    could not express is refused.
  - [x] ADFS unknown-metadata preservation. Opening a directory now captures
    everything the parser does not interpret — the bytes between the sequence
    byte and the first entry, the entry slots after the terminator, the tail
    beyond the fields this build writes, and each entry's whole ten-byte name
    field. That last one is the entirety of what an ADFS entry can carry
    unmodelled: ADFS ends a short name with a carriage return and leaves the
    rest of the field holding whatever the previous occupant of the slot left
    there, and a writer that padded it out would destroy all of it while still
    producing an image that mounts and lists correctly. Name fields are keyed by
    name rather than by position, so an entry that moves because a rename
    resorted the catalogue still finds its own bytes. A shrinking catalogue
    leaves an earlier remnant at the offset it had rather than sliding it, and
    where a larger catalogue must claim preserved bytes the count is returned
    and shown.
  - [x] ADFS write-back. Editing a catalogue entry no longer means rebuilding
    the image and losing the rest of the disc: a name, the load and execution
    addresses and the lock bit can be changed in place, through the preserving
    writer, in the browser-held image. Nothing moves, no length changes and the
    free-space map is never touched, which is why this is safe to offer without
    an allocator; the restraint is stated on the panel rather than left to be
    discovered. The result is parsed again before it is returned, and an image
    that would not parse is refused outright with the original left untouched
    rather than handed back with a warning.
  - [x] Cassette unknown-metadata preservation. A UEF is now read as the chunk
    list it is, and written back byte for byte when nothing has changed. The
    structure is all this build claims to know: no chunk's meaning is asserted,
    because a UEF carries origin text, inlay scans, position markers and tone
    data among other things and inventing a meaning for one would be inventing
    detail about somebody's tape. Every chunk is carried at the position it
    held, a trailing fragment after the last complete chunk survives, a
    truncated file is reported with the numbers rather than silently shortened,
    and a gzip-compressed UEF is named as compressed rather than as
    unrecognised. Mounting a UEF now shows what it actually contains, by chunk
    identifier and size.
  - [x] Evidence: 13 UEF chunk contracts, 13 ADFS directory preservation
    contracts, 12 ADFS edit contracts and 6 contracts on the editing panel
    itself — that it says what it will not touch before anything is changed,
    that a refused name or address disables the action rather than being
    reported after the fact, that only the fields that actually differ are sent
    (sending one back unchanged would still advance the directory's update
    sequence and tell a machine its cached copy was stale, which is a small lie
    with a real cost), and that a directory is offered no address fields
    because they mean nothing there. The two preservation claims were
    checked by deliberate breakage — removing the name-field preservation fails
    the round trip and the remnant contract, and passes everything else — and
    the edit contract proves an edit changes no byte of the image outside the
    single directory block it rewrites.

- [x] MED-303 Implement UEF/cassette input flow with timing/data and state-save

- [ ] MED-304 Implement ADFS image workflow for selected 8-bit/ARM profiles.
  The qualified A310 increment mounts exact 800 KiB D/E-format images, traverses

  Multi-file creation and catalogue write-back are now implemented and are

  The earlier D prototype was removed after FileCore rejected execution. Its E

  - [x] Multi-file ADFS E creation. The writer takes a whole disc's worth of
    files rather than one, laying each down in its own contiguous fragment in
    catalogue order with the remainder of the disc as a single free fragment.
    It never fragments a file and never reuses freed space, because nothing on a
    freshly written image has been freed — that restraint is why the allocation
    map and the catalogue cannot disagree. The catalogue is sorted by name, so
    the order files were handed over never shows up on the disc and two requests
    differing only in order produce identical images. A disc that will not fit
    says by how much, two names ADFS would not tell apart are refused, and every
    file is read back through the same parser the product uses on somebody
    else's disc before the image is handed over.
  - [x] The single-file path was proved byte-identical to the previous writer
    across three payload sizes before that transcription was retired, and the
    exact image that passed the RISC OS 3.11 execution gate is now pinned by
    digest. That evidence is about specific bytes, so a layout change has to
    announce itself rather than quietly retire the acceptance run.
  - [x] Catalogue write-back. See the MED-300 entry: a name, the load and
    execution addresses and the lock bit can be changed in place in the image
    open in the browser, through the preserving directory writer, with the
    result reparsed before it is returned and refused outright if it would not
    parse. Nothing moves, no length changes and the free-space map is never
    touched, which is why this needs no allocator.
- [x] MED-305 Implement the first qualified Atom media workflow selected from

  zero padding, little-endian 16-bit load/execute/length values and exact payload
  length. Executable RAM payloads can be handed to the real ROM-backed Atom;
  text ATM payloads are explicitly labelled for AtoMMC `*EXEC` and continue to

  port-8090 contract imported, renamed and rebuilt an eight-byte ATM, independently
  verified its bytes, then observed its live loop at PC `&2905` with no browser
  errors. AtoMMC hardware remains preview-only and is not falsely claimed as an
  emulated SD/MMC peripheral; AtomDOS remains a separately tracked target-profile
  qualification rather than a hidden dependency of this file workflow.
- [x] MED-306 Implement ROM image workflow with size/bank/header validation and
  explicit redistribution warnings. The browser-local vault validates exact

  images, reports repeated 16 KiB banks, inspects BBC language/service/type and
  copyright-header fields, stores SHA-256 provenance, supports atomic normalized
  folder import, and excludes ROM bytes from project exports. The UI explicitly
  states that structural validation proves neither authenticity nor permission
  and requires users to supply dumps they are legally entitled to use.
  - [x] Evidence: Covered by the ROM profile and adapter-support contracts,
   which prove a per-profile size mismatch, a wholly blank file and a blank

- [x] MED-307 Implement RISC OS application/disk transfer packaging preserving
  file types and application-directory semantics.
  - [x] Implement the first browser-local application-directory transfer for
    qualified RISC OS 3 profiles: lossless `!Run,feb` and `RunImage,ff8`, a
    separately versioned package schema, bounded HostFS staging, and one-click
    FileSwitch launch through the emulated keyboard.
  - [x] Extend the package model to complete application trees, `!Boot`,
    `!Sprites`, arbitrary typed resources, archives and ADFS transfer images.
    The package is now version 2 and holds any number of typed files at any
    depth, described only by their paths — the tree is derived where one is
    needed rather than stored a second time, because two descriptions of one
    structure are two things to keep correct. Names whose type RISC OS decides
    are enforced rather than assumed: `!Run` and `!Boot` must be Obey files and
    `!Sprites` must be a sprite file, since an application that breaks those
    conventions is broken in a way the Filer will not explain. Every path
    segment must also be a name ADFS can hold, so an application that stages to
    HostFS can also reach a disc, and a path that is a file in one entry and a
    directory prefix in another is refused rather than discovered when the disc
    writer tries to build it.
  - [x] ADFS transfer images and archives. `createApplicationDisc` writes the
    whole application directory, subdirectories and all, through the same ADFS E
    writer the media workspace uses, so the image is read back through this
    build's own parser before it is offered — the transfer path for a machine
    with no HostFS. `createApplicationArchive` stores each file under its
    `,xxx` name, which is how the filetypes survive a machine that knows nothing
    about RISC OS metadata; the archive is stored rather than compressed, so the
    same application always produces the same bytes.
  - [x] The A310 runtime's HostFS staging accepts the same tree: it creates each
    implied subdirectory shallowest-first, enforces the same decided types and
    the same both-a-file-and-a-directory refusal, and requires `!Run` and
    `RunImage` by name rather than requiring the application be exactly those
    two files.
  - [x] Evidence: 5 contracts on the resources panel covering the listing and
    what it marks as unremovable, a resource added at a path naming a

    disc holding both the build and a host file that both read back exactly, a
    case-insensitive name collision, an invalid filetype, a name the disc cannot
    hold, and no build being offered when there is not a current one.
  - [x] Evidence: 11 application-package contracts covering the tree, the
    decided types, collision and case refusals, removal of what makes it an
    application, the derived tree, an ADFS transfer disc whose resource is
    extracted byte for byte through the reader, and a reproducible archive.
- [x] MED-308 Implement disk sets, logical project files/artifacts, ordering,
  dependency build, boot/run profile, set export, and quota handling.
  - [x] A versioned `8bit-net.disk-set` document records which build artifacts,

  - [x] The set names the build targets it depends on, once each, in the order

  - [x] Capacity is answered from the real DFS geometry rather than a byte

  - [x] Writing produces every image in the set through the existing DFS and DSD

  - [x] A generated `!BOOT` entry writes `*RUN` for each other file on its side,

  - [x] Disk sets persist in the project at format `8bit-net-dev-project-19`; a

  - [x] Evidence: 15 unit contracts cover validation, the build plan, sector

### 5.4 Standalone file analysis

  - [x] Every media parser is now held to one blunt contract under seeded fuzz:
    for any input at all it must either return a structure satisfying its own
    declared invariants or throw an Error with a message, and it must never
    hang, read outside its input, or report an extent its image cannot hold.
    The DFS catalogue, ADFS catalogue, Atom ATM container, tape image, DFS image
    project and double-sided DFS project readers each run 120 random images
    across empty, single-byte, sub-sector, sector-aligned and real 200 KiB,
    400 KiB and 640 KiB sizes, plus single-bit mutations of a valid image.
  - [x] Round trips are property-tested rather than sampled: a written DFS image
    re-reads to the same title, boot option, addresses, lock flag and byte-exact
    payload; a double-sided image writes and re-reads both sides independently;
    an Atom ATM container round-trips its name, load address and payload.
  - [x] Reading past the end is tested directly. Truncating a valid image on a
    sector boundary makes an entry's extent unsatisfiable, and extraction
    refuses with that reason instead of returning a short read.
  - [x] Evidence: 29 contracts in `src/media/mediaParserProperties.test.ts`. No
    parser crashed or returned a malformed structure on any generated input.
- [x] ANL-300 Add bounded, browser-local file loading with filename, byte size,
  explicit analysis status and no implicit execution/upload (ANL-001).
  - [x] Evidence: 9 file-analysis contracts covering dialect routing, load and
   execute suffixes, Acorn and conventional hexadecimal addresses, binary
   routed to the ARM analyser without truncating a 26-bit address, and
   metadata precedence. Nothing is executed or uploaded: the analyser is a
   pure function over bytes held in the tab, which the contracts exercise
   directly.
- [x] ANL-301 Add conservative tokenized BBC BASIC II record validation,
  dialect-labelled listing, protected line-reference decoding, quoted-byte
  preservation, line-order warnings and trailing-payload accounting (ANL-004).
  - [x] Evidence: 4 tokenised BBC BASIC contracts covering ROM keyword
   decoding with tokens inside strings preserved, the protected three-byte

- [x] ANL-311 Add indirect-flow hints, extra entry points, code/data/text marking,
  comments, analysis undo/history and exact project persistence (ANL-008,
  ANL-010, ANL-012).
  - [x] A versioned annotation document, `8bit-net.analysis-annotations`, records
    what a reader knows that the bytes cannot say: extra entry points, spans
    marked code, data or text, the destinations of a jump the bytes do not
    resolve, comments and labels. It is bound to the SHA-256 of the bytes it
    describes, so annotations cannot silently follow a different file, and it is
    validated on every path in from outside the session — the project file, the
    analysis worker, an imported document — with a message naming exactly what
    is wrong rather than being repaired into something plausible.
  - [x] Every edit is a pure function returning a whole document, so undo
    restores the document that existed rather than a reconstruction of it. The
    history is bounded at sixty-four steps and says how many earlier steps it
    dropped instead of losing them silently.
  - [x] The analyser honours the record rather than guessing. A recorded entry
    point or code marking seeds decoding; a recorded destination is followed
    from whatever instruction sits at that address, which covers a jump through
    a pointer, a dispatch table and a computed return alike; a span marked data
    or text is kept out of the decoder entirely, and an instruction that would
    run into one is refused with a warning rather than decoded across it. A
    recorded label wins over every generated one, and a recorded comment is kept
    alongside the derived interpretation rather than replacing it.
  - [x] Marking overwrites rather than requiring an undo first: a new span trims
    or drops whatever it covers, so correcting an earlier decision is one step.
  - [x] Annotations persist in the project at format `8bit-net-dev-project-18`,
    keyed by digest so a binary keeps what was learned about it however it is
    reached, and travel in the exported analysis document so that document
    reproduces the listing rather than only describing it.
  - [x] Evidence: 41 unit contracts cover the document, the history and the
    analyser's use of both, including that an indirect jump is *not* followed
    when nothing is recorded. A headless Chromium run then drove the whole
    workflow in the built workbench: a binary whose only route to its tail is a
    jump through a pointer analysed with that tail as unreached data; recording
    the flow turned it into `LDA #&42` and made the jump row read `Recorded flow
    to location_1905`; the annotation appeared in the stored project; undo
    returned the listing to unreached data and redo brought it back; and naming
    the tail `pointer_tail` changed the jump row to `Recorded flow to
    pointer_tail`. Zero console errors.

- [x] ANL-313 Integrate static analysis with hex/source/debug/media/research

  - [x] Machine-code analysis now switches between the semantic listing and a

  - [x] A selected decoded instruction can open its exact mnemonic in the

  - [x] A selected static 6502/65C02 instruction can install an execute
    breakpoint in the connected ROM-backed emulator and open the real Debugger.
