    preserving the static/runtime evidence boundary.
  - [x] A static listing can now be correlated with what the machine was
    observed executing, without the two being merged. The correlation is
    refused unless the running program's SHA-256 is the digest of the analysed
    bytes and it was loaded at the address the listing assumes; each refusal
    names its own reason, including which other program is running or where the
    same bytes were loaded instead. When it is bound, each row reports the
    instruction and cycle counts the live profiler actually recorded, and rows
    it never saw say only that they were not observed, because runtime silence
    is not evidence of anything. Rows the analysis called reachable but the
    machine never reached, and rows the machine executed that static analysis
    did not call code, are counted separately and shown, since each is a real
    finding. Profiler samples outside the analysed file and the profiler's own
    unattributed instruction count are reported rather than dropped. 12
    contracts cover the refusals and the arithmetic.
- [ ] ANL-314 Move heavy analysis into a cancellable bounded worker, add
  progress/virtualization and adversarial parser performance tests (ANL-016).
  - [x] All standalone and media-origin analysis now runs in a fresh module
    worker rather than the React/UI thread. A superseding request or explicit
    Cancel terminates the worker; stale results are rejected by task identity;
    a hard 20-second ceiling terminates pathological input; errors are visible.
    Status reports real stages/byte counts without fabricated percentages, and
    input transfer uses a bounded copy so the retained immutable payload is not
    detached. The hex view is fixed-window. Fine-grained parser progress keeps
    the parent open.
  - [x] The adversarial performance corpus exists, and it found two real
    defects on its first run. The property tests already proved these readers
    either parse random bytes or refuse them, which says nothing about what
    they cost: a reader that is correct on every input and quadratic on one of
    them still exhausts the worker's twenty-second ceiling, which turns a hang
    into a refusal — the right behaviour and the wrong outcome, because the
    person gets nothing. Fifteen cases now feed each reader the input built to
    be its worst: a bank of one-byte instructions, a bank of nothing but calls,
    a bank of undefined opcodes, a bank of branches that all land on one byte,
    a chain of ARM branches each reaching a different word, a run of ARM
    conditional branches all naming the same word, a search
    pattern that matches at every offset, the near-miss pattern that is the
    classic worst case for a plain scan, two images differing at every byte, a
    program of the shortest lines the format can express, and disc images built
    to look plausible for as long as a reader will let them. Each case asserts
    the reader did the work as well as how long it took, because a case that
    quietly produced nothing would pass a timing check while proving nothing.
  - [x] **Two quadratic paths in the 6502 reader, found by that corpus and
    fixed.** A 64 KiB image of nothing but branches took 5.9 seconds. Its work
    queue was walked by removing the first element of an array, which costs
    what is left in it, and a bank of branches puts one entry there per
    instruction; and every control-flow target searched the finished rows for
    its own address, which is proportional to the square of the listing. The
    queue is now walked by index and the rows are indexed once by address. The
    same image now takes 0.73 seconds — eight times faster, and the change is
    order-preserving, so every existing disassembly contract still passes
    unchanged.
  - [x] **A worse one in the ARM reader, past the ceiling the product would
    have refused it at.** A 128 KiB image of conditional branches all naming one
    word took twenty-two seconds — longer than the analysis worker's own
    twenty-second limit, so a real file of that shape would have been abandoned
    rather than read. A conditional branch does not end a run, so every one of
    those decoded and every one recorded a reference against the same address,
    and the reference list was rebuilt from scratch each time. It is now
    appended to in place, and the work queue is walked by index like the 6502
    reader's. The same image now takes under four tenths of a second. The first
    ARM case written for this corpus did not find either defect, because a run
    of unconditional branches stops at the first one and decodes almost
    nothing: a case that looks adversarial and exercises nothing proves nothing,
    so both ARM cases now assert how many instructions were actually decoded.
- [x] ANL-315 Complete keyboard, screen-reader, forced-colour, 200% zoom and
  320 CSS-pixel reflow acceptance for the analysis journey (ANL-018).
  - [x] The disassembly listing is a real grid rather than a list of buttons.
    Its columns are named for a screen reader instead of being hidden from it,
    each row reports its own index and whether it is selected, and only one row
    is in the tab order, so a listing running to thousands of rows is not
    thousands of tab stops. Arrow keys move between rows, Page Up and Page Down
    move by a screenful, Home and End reach the ends, and movement stops at
    either end rather than wrapping. Enter and Space activate the focused row.
  - [x] A branch or call target is a real button with an accessible name, not a
    span with a link role nested inside a button, which was invalid markup and
    unreachable in the previous keyboard order.
  - [x] Forced colours are honoured for the listing, its selection outline, the
    coverage strip, the annotation controls and the adapter capability note, so
    none of them rely on a colour the system has replaced.
  - [x] The layout reflows rather than clipping: below 900 CSS pixels the
    inspector moves under the listing instead of competing with it for width,
    and below 620 the observed-execution column drops before the instruction it
    describes does. That covers 200% zoom on a 640-pixel-wide viewport and a
    320-pixel viewport.
  - [x] 11 acceptance contracts cover the grid semantics, the keyboard model,
    the accessible names of the annotation controls, and that the coverage
    column is absent until the correlation is bound rather than shown empty.

### Phase 3 exit gate

- [ ] BUILD-GATE First machine slice builds reproducibly through a sandbox,
  reports navigable diagnostics/symbols/source maps, creates independently
  validated authentic media, and exports a portable project/artifact bundle.

## 6. Phase 4 — emulator and complete runtime

### 6.1 Shared emulator framework

Current implemented increment:

- [x] Processor-independent runtime boundary is exercised by a bounded ROM-less
  6502 diagnostic adapter with load, run, instruction-step, reset, snapshot,
  memory, trace, breakpoint and explicit fault/halt states.
- [x] Runtime console captures the deliberately supported OSWRCH/OSASCI/OSNEWL
  development shims and visibly states that video/audio/input and machine
  hardware are not emulated by this adapter.
- [x] Pinned jsbeeb 1.19.1 is built as an isolated `emulator.html` surface with
  same-origin message validation and no bundled ROMs; its GPL licence is served
  from the production container.
- [x] Origin-private IndexedDB ROM manifests validate, hash, store, remove and
  serve BBC B, Master and Atom firmware through a scoped service-worker route.
- [x] A normalized-folder importer path-matches and size-validates the selected
  engine manifest, rejects missing required or ambiguous inputs before writing,
  and commits every accepted ROM through one IndexedDB transaction. Engine ID
  and pinned version are explicit in the UI; BBC A and Electron remain gated
  because jsbeeb 1.19.1 has no truthful hardware model for either profile.
- [x] BBC Model B boots real MOS 1.20, DFS and BASIC from the local ROM vault,
  renders its framebuffer, accepts keyboard events, and exposes run, pause,
  reset and exact instruction-step controls.
- [x] Tokenized BBC BASIC builds load at PAGE and run inside the real BBC Model B
  through the emulated keyboard; a browser contract verifies screen output.
- [x] Successful 6502/65C12 builds can load into a bounded live RAM range on a
  ROM-aware target, set the real CPU entry point, start or pause for debugging,
  and install source-line breakpoints resolved through the build source map. A
  production browser contract verifies the bytes, PC and hardware single-step.
- [x] BBC Master MOS 3.20 boots from the MAME-derived local image, renders its
  framebuffer, runs the supported BBC BASIC token subset, and exposes the same
  live register/step debugger contract.
- [x] Acorn Atom boots to its real ROM prompt after correctly splitting MAME's
  combined `abasic.ic20` BASIC/kernel image; framebuffer and live CPU stepping
  are browser-tested. Atom BASIC is built as validated numbered text and entered
  through the emulated 8255 keyboard matrix; a deployed end-to-end browser test
  proves `NEW`, source entry, `RUN` and visible program output on the real ROM.
- [x] The pinned, ROM-free Arculator WASM core qualifies the first A310/ARM2
  slice with browser-local four-lane RISC OS 3.11 normalization, genuine 1 MiB
  machine configuration, framebuffer, reset, pause, exact instruction step,
  all 16 live registers, flags/mode, mapped memory and ARM2 disassembly.
- [x] A bounded authentic/fast-boot choice is explicit. Fast boot advances real
  emulated time, and the empty-breakpoint scan is removed from the tens-of-
  millions-of-instructions boot hot path; the measured five-second boot segment
  fell from 13.7 s to 4.0 s on the development host without skipping core work.
  The runtime now consumes at most 100 emulated milliseconds per animation
  frame, publishes firmware/runtime/filesystem/hardware/boot phases and bounded
  progress, and reads origin-private firmware directly from IndexedDB. A
  60-second emulated boot completed in 24.4 seconds wall time without runtime
  errors while remaining responsive to browser progress frames.
- [x] The A310 debugger installs real execute breakpoints only when requested.
  A raw ARM2 build can be loaded into the paused mapped range
  `&00008000–&000FFFFF`, read back byte-for-byte, handed to the real 26-bit PC
  pipeline, stopped at a source-resolved breakpoint and single-stepped. The UI
  identifies this as a raw debug image, not a packaged RISC OS application.
- [x] The first RISC OS 3 application launch path uses the pinned GPL support
  extension ROM and its Emscripten HostFS backend. It stages an independently
  typed application directory, injects F12 and the exact FileSwitch `Run`
  command through the emulated keyboard controller, and reports success only
  after a non-pausing core execution observer sees the Absolute entry at
  `&00008000`. A headed Chromium acceptance run on port 8090 observed staging,
  command entry and genuine ARM execution with zero browser/runtime errors.
- [x] The A310 adapter now exposes Arculator's live SDL/VIDC audio lifecycle,
  starts muted, supports explicit browser activation, and reports core enable
  state plus SDL queued bytes. Its framebuffer is encoded directly to PNG, and
  exact 800 KiB ADFS `.adf` images mount through the upstream floppy-change
  path into drive 0 or 1. One headed Chromium run on port 8090 proved typed
  application launch, audio enable/mute, an 819,200-byte drive-1 attachment
  acknowledged by the core, and a non-empty PNG with zero runtime errors.
- [x] Exact ADFS D/E imports now receive a read-only recursive catalogue before
  mounting. The parser follows FileCore's 2 KiB new-directory structure and
  checksum algorithm, validates both `Nick` signatures, D old-map or one-zone E
  new-map geometry and checksums, verifies the declared 800 KiB geometry and
  the D-format bounded free-space table, and shows
  title/name/sequence plus object attributes, load/execute addresses, lengths,
  filetypes and indirect disc addresses. D sector extents and E fragment chains
  resolve to exact downloadable file bytes; recursion is bounded by depth,
  object count and cycle detection. Invalid checksums are rejected rather than
  presenting damaged directory data as trustworthy. Editing and image creation
  remain under MED-304. The File Forge `samples/Example.adf` E-format fixture
  parses with no warnings and extracts `$.TextFile` as `here is some text`.
- [x] A headed Chromium/Arculator acceptance on port 8090 mounted that real
  File Forge ADFS E fixture in drive 1. The live bridge reported `ADFS E 800K
  ADF`, Arculator reported the controller loaded and retained all 819,200 bytes,
  and both browser and runtime error streams were empty.
- [x] The one-file ADFS E writer emits a standard one-zone disc record, duplicate
  allocation maps, bounded fragment/free-space chain, CR-padded `Nick` root,
  executable access and typed &FF8 metadata, then independently reparses and
  byte-compares the file before enabling download or mount. A headed RISC OS
  3.11 run mounted the WebIDE-produced 819,200-byte image in drive 0, entered
  `Run ADFS::0.$.DiscDemo` through the emulated keyboard, and the live ARM core
  observed execution at &00008000 with no browser/runtime errors. Automatic
  generated-image launch remains qualified only on drive 0; drive 1 mounting
  is supported but its direct execution path has not passed this gate.
- [x] Safe A310 save/restore feasibility has been bounded and surfaced honestly.
  Pinned Arculator revision `579ac437b9a4` exposes no complete serialization
  contract. Dumping WebAssembly linear memory would omit SDL/browser objects,
  mounted Emscripten files and external device state while restoring stale
  handles, so A310 save/load controls remain disabled until the core supplies a
  deterministic component-level snapshot API. This limitation is not presented
  as machine-state support.
- [x] Tube capability selection now makes the correct 6502 or 65C102 boot ROM a
  conditional firmware requirement; a BBC B + 6502 Tube host boot is exercised
  with the capability reflected in the live runtime title.
- [x] The development 1MHzPi BBC WiFi ROM is a conditional, explicitly
  development-status firmware requirement on the qualified BBC B and Master
  profiles, and is recorded as provisionally applicable to BBC B+ pending a
  qualified B+ machine adapter. Once supplied through the local vault, enabling the capability
  passes only its manifest-owned path to jsbeeb's real `extraRoms` loader and
  places it in a non-RAM sideways bank; Tube firmware is not mistaken for a
  sideways ROM. The same checkbox is visible but preview-only for BBC B+ and
  Electron because neither has a qualified jsbeeb machine adapter. Runtime
  commands now require the actual parent frame and reject traversal, absolute,
  oversized or excessive extra-ROM manifests. Registry contracts cover disabled,
  Tube-only, enabled and shared BBC/Master paths. A deployed Chromium contract
  imports the exact local development snapshot, observes its HTTP 200 vault
  request, and reads its first byte back from physical sideways bank 13 with
  zero browser errors. That test caught and fixed jsbeeb's convenience factory
  dropping `extraRoms` before initialization. The Plus 1 RH and modified
  ElkWiFi ROMs are separately inventoried from the 1MHzPi project as moving
  development firmware. When Electron + Plus 1 and the 1MHzPi capability are
  selected, the local firmware workspace accepts and hashes both independent
  payloads, requires the raw 16 KiB ElkWiFi image rather than its 16,406-byte
  Acorn file-header form, and deliberately leaves runtime readiness false.
  Mounting both ROMs and emulating the live external 1 MHz bus remain open and
  must not be inferred from the working BBC WiFi ROM path. The same evolving
  1MHz WiFi firmware is provisionally catalogued for BBC B, B+ and Master, with
  B+ runtime qualification still open.
- [x] Versioned jsbeeb v3 state files can be downloaded and restored through the
  isolated runtime. CPU/RAM/paging/scheduler/video/sound/VIA/ACIA/ADC/FDC and
  Tube state use the emulator's native snapshot contract; bounded locally
  mounted disk images are embedded, ROMs are excluded, restore pauses and
  repaints, and model/Tube mismatches are rejected. A production browser test
  proves an exact PC/media round trip. Persistent state library, quota UI and
  rewind remain open under EMU-405.
- [x] The live 1024×625 emulator framebuffer can be downloaded directly as PNG
  without a server upload, and the machine surface supports browser full-screen
  with an explicit accessible in-frame exit control. A production browser test
  validates the PNG signature/dimensions and full viewport geometry. Audio/frame
  recording and deterministic capture modes remain open under EMU-406.
- [x] BBC SN76489 and Atom speaker audio now use jsbeeb's real sound-chip path
  and isolated AudioWorklet renderer instead of `FakeSoundChip`. Audio is muted
  by default, requires explicit enablement, has an in-frame autoplay fallback,
  reports availability/context/buffer/signal state, and suspends on mute. A
  production browser contract runs BBC BASIC `SOUND`, observes 267 nonzero chip
  buffers, and verifies deterministic initial/final mute. Volume, latency/
  underrun UI, drive noise, Music 5000 and audio recording remain open.

- [x] EMU-400 Finalize lifecycle/capability/state/input/debug adapter contracts
  from EMU-001 and contract-test fake adapters. Emulator adapter API 1 now
  defines typed configuration, exact manifest and ROM provenance, media,
  artifact, reset, input, state, capture and inspection payloads plus every
  required lifecycle operation. A shared transition guard rejects illegal
  state changes, capability guards reject unavailable calls, and descriptor
  validation requires all operations and honest limitations. Complete and
  deliberately partial fake adapters exercise the lifecycle, stepping, input,
  capture, debug, provenance-safe restore, destruction and failure paths. The
  pinned jsbeeb and Arculator declarations pass the same validation and their
  exact yes/no operation matrix is visible in the live Debug session panel.
- [x] EMU-401 Run emulators in isolated workers/frames/processes with explicit
  CSP/origin/message validation, bounded queues, cleanup, and crash recovery.
  jsbeeb and Arculator run only in sandboxed same-origin frames with separate
  embedded-document CSP. The parent and both runtimes validate exact origin,
  source window, channel, random session nonce and monotonic sequence before
  accepting a message. One command may be in flight and at most 64 may wait;
  overflow is refused visibly. Frame identity changes clear pending work,
  counters, state ownership and acknowledgements. Page teardown now cancels
  animation or fast-boot work, timers, hooks, pending tests, audio and core
  execution. A visible Restart adapter action replaces a failed frame and
  rotates its nonce. Protocol tests cover wrong-session and stale rejection;
  deployed BBC and A310 contracts have also proved forged messages cannot reset
  either core.
- [x] EMU-402 Bind every session to immutable build/profile/ROM/run manifests
  and display provenance plus substitutions/limitations (EMU-003).
  - [x] Bind normal jsbeeb and A310 boot sessions to a versioned immutable
    runtime manifest. The normalized declaration includes a random session ID,
    creation time, pinned adapter revision, exact platform/machine/variant/model
    and ROM-set IDs, sorted capabilities, Tube and boot options, selected
    sideways-ROM paths, every required browser-local ROM filename/size/SHA-256,
    substitutions and limitations. A dependency-free SHA-256 fingerprints the
    entire declaration. The jsbeeb child fully revalidates the fingerprint and
    resolved model before initialization; A310 validates the bounded identity,
    adapter, profile and fingerprint contract. Both echo the manifest in every
    snapshot and the parent refuses mismatched state. The disclosure displays
    complete ROM digests and limitations without exposing ROM bytes. Unit tests
    cover normalization, deep freezing, invalid ROM records and fingerprint
    tampering. A headed deployed Chromium gate independently hashed all three
    BBC Model B source ROM files, matched the live child manifest, then forged a
    same-origin, same-session snapshot with a different fingerprint and observed
    parent rejection. No browser errors or failed responses occurred.
  - [x] Bind assembled 6502, raw ARM2 and Atom ATM program loads to the current
    runtime session. The parent verifies the exact build or host-file SHA-256,
    target, pinned toolchain, mode, processor, origin, entry point and byte count,
    then fingerprints the complete program declaration with the current SESSION
    fingerprint. jsbeeb reconstructs and validates that declaration before RAM
    writes. A310 independently hashes the bytes and canonical declaration with
    Web Crypto before RAM writes. Both children return PROGRAM with
    program-loaded and every later snapshot; the parent refuses a cross-session
    program echo. The live disclosure exposes complete digests and exports a
    versioned 8bit-net.runtime-run JSON record that fingerprints the complete
    session and program manifests. Unit tests cover exact binding, byte,
    fingerprint and cross-session tampering. A headed deployed Chromium gate
    built and ran an eight-byte 6502 program, independently matched its SHA-256,
    matched the child snapshot to the active session, inspected the full visible
    digest and produced the maintained help screenshot with no browser errors or
    failed requests. Hardware tests now use the same contract with a distinct
    test mode: the parent binds the exact rebuilt bytes, the child rejects a
    mismatched result/build fingerprint, PROGRAM appears after acceptance and
    the stopped result carries its program fingerprint. A deployed headed UI
    contract built a ten-byte 6502 target, passed X=5 at the declared symbol,
    matched PROGRAM across load, result and snapshot, and exported a complete
    fingerprinted runtime-run record under one random session with no browser or
    HTTP errors. Tokenized BBC BASIC and keyboard-entered Atom BASIC now use the
    same byte and session binding with explicit placement policies. BBC BASIC
    declares interpreter PAGE placement and reports the real resolved PAGE on
    acceptance. Atom BASIC declares paced keyboard-queue placement without
    inventing an address before the guest interpreter consumes it. Separate
    deployed headed contracts built and loaded a 26-byte BBC token stream and a
    29-byte Atom numbered-text payload, matched PROGRAM into later snapshots and
    exported valid fingerprinted run records under one session each with no
    browser errors or failed requests. This completes EMU-402.
- [x] EMU-403 Implement runtime surface and controls: build/load/run, pause,
  reset/power, speed, scaling/full-screen, audio, input, and media.
  - [x] Add validated, origin-persisted Fit, 1x and 2x framebuffer modes. Fit
    remains fluid; jsbeeb integer modes use its real 1,024 by 625 render extent;
    A310 modes use the live SDL canvas dimensions reported in the VIDC snapshot.
    Oversized integer viewports scroll rather than silently shrink. Browser
    full screen temporarily overrides retained integer dimensions so the whole
    display remains accessible, while PNG capture continues to use real canvas
    pixels. Model tests cover exact multiplication and invalid live dimensions.
    Maintained technical help includes a dedicated procedure and deployed
    screenshot. Authentic display effects and complete guest media write-back
    controls remain open, so EMU-403 is not complete.
  - [x] Add real full-machine power lifecycle and qualified jsbeeb runtime
    pacing. Power off unmounts the sandboxed emulator iframe and clears volatile
    RAM ownership, debugger views, pending transport, input state, audio state,
    tests and media acknowledgements. Power on increments the frame generation,
    rotates the random session nonce and boots from the configured ROMs. The
    jsbeeb adapter validates 0.5x, 1x, 2x and 4x, multiplies its real per-frame
    CPU cycle budget and publishes speed-state plus the accepted rate in every
    snapshot. Audio is mutually excluded from non-authentic rates because pitch
    and buffering outside 1x are not qualified. A310 speed remains visibly
    disabled because the pinned core exposes no safe live pacing API. A headed
    deployed Chromium gate observed 2x from the live child, proved the iframe
    disappeared on power off, proved Run was disabled, then observed a new
    random session and retained 2x acknowledgement after power on. It reported
    no browser errors or failed responses and produced the maintained technical
    help screenshot.
  - [x] Add acknowledged jsbeeb master volume plus real jsbeeb and A310 canvas
    filtering. Volume is a validated whole percentage from 0 to 100 and changes
    the real WebAudio GainNode. The child returns volume-state only after
    mutation and includes volume in audio diagnostics. A310 volume remains
    visibly unavailable because its pinned SDL path exposes sound enable and
    queue depth but no qualified master gain. PIXEL applies pixelated
    nearest-neighbour sampling; SMOOTH applies browser linear interpolation on
    the actual emulator canvas. Both children validate and acknowledge the
    filter, and origin-local choices are reapplied after a new child reports
    ready. CSS filtering remains separate from framebuffer size and PNG source
    pixels. A headed deployed Chromium gate observed 37 percent in both
    volume-state and audio-state, directly inspected auto then pixelated on the
    child canvas, verified persisted controls and reported no browser errors or
    failed responses. Maintained technical help documents mute, zero gain,
    autoplay, speed, scaling, capture and A310 boundaries.
  - [x] Add live jsbeeb disk and cassette eject. Disk eject validates drive 0
    or 1, calls the real FDC with an empty image and removes only that retained
    drive. Cassette eject clears the real ACIA or Atom PPIA tape source. Both
    operations reset the replay segment and publish media-ejected only after
    mutation; the parent removes its acknowledgement only on that child event.
    A310 eject remains visibly disabled because the pinned core has no
    qualified unload contract. A headed deployed Chromium journey created and
    independently reopened a 204,800-byte blank DFS SSD, mounted it in live
    drive 0, observed media-loaded, captured the maintained help screenshot,
    ejected it, observed media-ejected from the same random session and reached
    the empty state with no browser errors or failed responses. Guest write-back
    qualification is recorded in the following child item.
  - [x] Add qualified jsbeeb SSD and DSD guest write tracking and current-image
    export. Mount now supplies the pinned upstream complete-image change
    callback. Each callback replaces the retained child bytes, marks only that
    drive guest-modified, increments a visible revision and publishes bounded
    metadata. Export current asks the active child for a Blob of those retained
    bytes instead of downloading the stale host source; export does not clear
    dirty state. A310 and jsbeeb ADF/ADL remain explicitly unavailable because
    their pinned adapters do not expose qualified current-byte callbacks. A
    headed deployed Chromium gate mounted an independently built 204,800-byte
    blank SSD, entered a real DFS *SAVE through the BBC keyboard and MOS, saw
    live write revision 1, exported the current image and independently proved
    its SHA-256 differed from the mounted source while geometry stayed exact.
    The maintained technical help screenshot, zero browser errors and zero
    failed requests are recorded.
  - [x] Add CLEAN, LINES and SOFT CRT presentation modes without changing the
    emulated framebuffer. LINES is a pointer-transparent CSS scanline overlay;
    SOFT CRT adds token-driven grille and vignette gradients. The selector and
    technical help explicitly reject hardware-accuracy claims and distinguish
    effects from pixel filtering, framebuffer scaling and PNG capture. Effect
    colours live in the separate theme token sheet and can be overridden by an
    8bit-net deployment without changing component geometry. The validated
    browser-origin preference survives reload. Unit tests bound the catalogue
    and reject misleading stored values. A headed deployed Chromium gate
    inspected the real pseudo-element gradients and pointer policy, captured a
    paused framebuffer before and after SOFT CRT, independently matched both PNG
    SHA-256 values, reloaded and proved persistence with no browser errors or
    failed requests. Together with the qualified build/load/run, execution,
    reset, power, speed, scaling, full-screen, audio, input, media mount/eject
    and guest SSD/DSD export paths above, this completes EMU-403. Per-adapter
    limitations remain visible rather than being treated as missing controls.
- [ ] EMU-404 Implement keyboard viewer/remapper, focus capture/release, pasted
  text policy, browser-shortcut conflicts, gamepad/joystick/mouse/analogue maps.
  - [x] Add a first real keyboard-input slice for jsbeeb and A310. A sequenced,
    session-bound adapter command now captures or releases canvas focus and the
    child reports the resulting focus state. The jsbeeb runtime validates and
    persists Physical, Natural or Gaming mapping profiles, exposes an
    accessible Acorn key surface backed by maintained host key identities, and
    queues at most 4,096 reviewed ASCII characters through the Atom or BBC
    keyboard converter. The A310 queues its supported subset through the real
    SDL host-key bridge; its on-screen keys remain visibly disabled pending a
    complete verified scancode map. Browser conflict policy and replay
    boundaries are stated in the interface and maintained help. A headed
    deployed Chromium gate imported the non-git ROM folder, booted BBC Model B,
    observed same-session focus, release, gaming-layout, A-key and 22-character
    acknowledgements, persisted the layout, captured the help screenshot and
    reported no browser errors or failed responses. Custom rebinding evidence
    follows; gamepad, joystick, mouse and analogue input remain open.
  - [x] Add bounded custom jsbeeb host-key rebinding. The editor offers only
    maintained navigation, editing, letter and function-key identities and the
    maintained accessible Acorn surface. Validation limits tables to 32, rejects
    unknown host and target codes and prevents duplicate host identities. Add or
    replace, per-row remove and clear operations persist only validated sorted
    tables, clear held matrix state and wait for child acknowledgement. The live
    canvas translates both keydown and keyup before calling jsbeeb and publishes
    a session-bound audit event for each remapped edge. Unit tests cover sorting,
    duplicates and invalid identities. A headed deployed Chromium gate mapped
    host A to Acorn SPACE, observed the acknowledged table, delivered both live
    matrix edges under one random session, reloaded and proved persistence with
    no browser errors or failed requests. Maintained technical help records the
    browser-shortcut and local-preference boundaries. Gamepad evidence follows;
    native joystick interfaces, mouse and unrestricted analogue input remain
    open.
  - [x] Add standard browser gamepad polling mapped to real held jsbeeb key
    edges. The validated origin-local configuration selects one of four browser
    controller indexes, applies a 0.10 to 0.90 axis dead zone and maps standard
    axes 0/1, D-pad buttons 12 to 15 and buttons 0/1 onto six unique maintained
    Acorn key identities. Unique targets prevent one action release from
    releasing another active action. The parent polls at presentation cadence
    but sends only changed edges; cleanup, disable, configuration change,
    disconnect and machine loss release every active action. The child validates
    action, target and edge before calling the real jsbeeb keyboard path and
    returns a session-bound acknowledgement. The accessible UI reports exact
    controller identity, button count and held actions. Unit tests cover axes,
    D-pad, fire buttons, thresholds and invalid mappings. A headed deployed
    Chromium gate presented a standard controller, held axis-left and button 0,
    observed live LEFT and Z presses, disconnected, observed both releases under
    the same random runtime session and verified persisted configuration with no
    browser errors or failed requests. Native joystick interfaces, mouse and
    unrestricted analogue input remain open, so EMU-404 is not complete.
  - [x] Add a native BBC analogue joystick mode beside key mapping. The validated
    saved gamepad configuration now selects key edges or BBC analogue hardware,
    while older records migrate to keys. On BBC A, BBC B, BBC B+ and Master,
    axes 0 through 3 are dead-zone centred, range-clamped, inverted to the real
    16-bit BBC convention and installed as jsbeeb ADC channel sources. Buttons 0
    and 1 drive the real active-low System VIA PB4 and PB5 joystick fire inputs.
    The runtime validates all four channel words and two booleans, reads the
    values back from the installed ADC sources and System VIA joystick latches,
    publishes a same-session hardware acknowledgement and creates a replay boundary.
    Disconnect, disable, configuration change and machine loss centre the ADC
    and release both buttons. Unit tests cover inversion, centring, buttons,
    interface migration and rejection. A deployed headed Chromium contract
    supplies exact controller state, verifies exact ADC and System VIA readback,
    verifies disconnect reset under the same random session, and captures
    maintained native-interface help evidence without browser or HTTP errors.
    Atom, Electron and A310 native joystick devices, 6502 mouse expansions and
    less bounded analogue peripherals remain open, so EMU-404 is not complete.
  - [x] Add a mutually exclusive BBC and Master mouse-driven analogue joystick.
    The origin-persisted control disables browser gamepad polling before taking
    ownership of the shared connector. Pointer position maps the complete live
    canvas to ADC channels 0 and 1 using the real inverted 16-bit convention;
    channels 2 and 3 remain centred. Left and right buttons drive System VIA PB4
    and PB5. Each delivered state is checked against the installed ADC sources
    and VIA latches. Pointer leave, blur, Release input, reset and disable centre
    all channels and release fire, while right click is consumed only when the
    mode is active. A deployed headed Chromium contract read back ADC 49,151 and
    16,384 at a declared canvas position, read back Fire 1, then moved to the
    parent status bar and proved exact centring and release under the same random
    session. It also proved persisted mutual exclusion and captured maintained
    help evidence with no browser errors or failed requests. This is explicitly
    an analogue-joystick mapping, not an AMX Mouse or guest pointer peripheral.
    Electron and A310 native joystick devices plus 6502 mouse expansions
    remain open, so EMU-404 is not complete.
  - [x] Add genuine A310 absolute mouse input through the pinned Arculator core.
    The browser child scales client coordinates to the live canvas backing,
    clamps them and maps left, right and middle to Arculator's existing 1, 2 and
    4 button bits. A bounded C bridge merges those values in
    `mouse_poll_host`; the core then consumes them through its existing
    `doosmouse` path. Pointer leave, blur, Release input, reset and page teardown
    clear injected buttons. The accessible KEYS panel reports exact live
    coordinates, framebuffer bounds and button mask. Qualification also fixed a
    readiness ownership regression where the generic ROM manager could
    overwrite a valid Archimedes physical-lane result. A deployed headed
    Chromium contract imports the MAME A310 set, waits through genuine core
    initialisation, verifies movement plus left and right edges under one random
    session, verifies release on pointer leave, captures maintained help evidence
    and reports browser errors and failed requests. Relative pointer lock,
    native joystick interfaces, 6502-family mouse expansions and unrestricted
    analogue input remain open, so EMU-404 is not complete.
  - [x] Add the native Atom AtoMMC joystick path beside key mapping. The target
    exposes it only when the immutable runtime manifest enables AtoMMC. Standard
    axes or D-pad directions map to AtomMMC2 gamepad indices 12 through 15 and
    either fire button maps to its single index 0 input. The real device exposes
    these as active-low right, left, down, up and fire bits through CMD_READ_PORT
    at &B400. Disconnect, disable, interface change and session loss restore &FF.
    Portable Tests retain a separate five-boolean AtoMMC action and runtime
    validation refuses it outside a qualified Atom AtoMMC session. A deployed
    headed Chromium contract applied U/L/F, executed guest 6502 code that wrote
    &A2 then read &B400, observed accumulator &E5 and passed at the exact stop
    address in 10 cycles. The same session presented a standard controller with
    D/L/F as live port &E9, disconnected it and observed &FF, with no browser
    errors or failed requests. Electron and A310 native joysticks plus other Atom
    adapters and 6502 mouse expansions remain open, so EMU-404 is not complete.
- [x] EMU-405 Implement versioned save/load state with compatibility checks and
  storage quotas; add rewind only after deterministic snapshot/replay proof.
  - [x] Replace raw jsbeeb state downloads with schema version 1
    8bit-net.machine-state envelopes. Each file binds the exact jsbeeb revision,
    model, ROM-set ID, Tube and sorted sideways-ROM configuration, plus every
    required ROM storage key, byte count and SHA-256. The embedded upstream
    snapshot records its UTF-8 byte count and SHA-256. Restore enforces the
    12 MiB limit and validates every compatibility and integrity field before
    parsing the jsbeeb payload. Legacy raw snapshots, wrong adapters, profiles,
    ROM bytes and tampered payloads are refused. Mounted disks retain separate
    drive and 2 MiB bounds, and successful restore remains paused and resets
    unsafe debugger/replay history. Unit tests cover round trip, legacy, wrong
    model, wrong ROM and payload tampering. A headed deployed Chromium journey
    downloaded a 180,869-byte state with three ROM digests, independently
    recomputed its 179,497-byte payload SHA-256, reset, restored it with the same
    child acknowledgement and proved a one-character payload mutation was
    refused. No browser errors or failed responses occurred. A310 state remains
    disabled with its complete-core reason.
  - [x] Add live browser-origin storage usage, quota, percentage and retention
    reporting to Settings. Values come directly from StorageManager estimate
    and persisted APIs; optional usage categories remain browser-labelled. An
    80 percent warning names the export-first recovery policy. A deliberate
    user control calls the browser persistence request and reports grant or
    denial without claiming control over browser policy. The panel makes clear
    that project, preference and private ROM data use the origin quota while
    downloaded portable projects and machine-state files do not. A headed
    deployed Chromium gate matched displayed values to 2,705 live bytes,
    1,829,794,671 quota bytes, best-effort retention and the exact IndexedDB and
    service-worker categories, then refreshed cleanly with no browser errors or
    failed responses. DBG-543 already provides bounded deterministic jsbeeb
    replay with verified checkpoints, forward verification, memory budgets and
    explicit irreversible boundaries. A310 remains excluded because it has no
    qualifying complete snapshot contract.
- [x] EMU-406 Implement screenshot/frame/audio capture and deterministic muted
  headless operation.
  - [x] Add bounded user-facing jsbeeb WAV capture. Start requires enabled audio
    at authentic 1x and retains copies from the real Float32 sound-chip callback
    up to a validated 1 to 30 second sample budget; the toolbar requests 10.
    Stop concatenates retained PCM, clamps finite samples and emits standard
    mono little-endian PCM16 RIFF/WAVE with exact header and data sizes. Capture
    is deliberately before the WebIDE GainNode, while Tests retains its separate
    deterministic speaker-free SN76489 write digest. The active child reports
    recording state, maximum samples, sample rate, final samples, duration and
    bytes. A headed deployed Chromium gate activated real audio, recorded and
    downloaded a 273,452-byte WAV, independently verified RIFF/WAVE/data,
    mono, 16 bits, 48 kHz and 273,408 PCM bytes, and matched the child's 136,704
    samples and 2.848-second acknowledgement. No browser errors or failed
    responses occurred. The A310 bridge now captures the exact post-mix
    interleaved stereo frames immediately before the pinned core queues them to
    SDL. A fixed 1 to 30 second C allocation bounds retention; stop copies only
    completed frames out of WASM, releases the allocation and emits 48 kHz
    stereo PCM16 with exact frame, sample, duration, channel and byte metadata.
    The parent preserves requested audio intent separately from asynchronous SDL
    device state, routes the Archimedes Blob through the real download path and
    accepts the display filter safely before core initialization completes. A
    deployed Chromium gate downloaded a 748,844-byte file containing 187,200
    stereo frames, independently verified RIFF/WAVE, format 1, two channels,
    48 kHz, 16 bits, 192,000 byte/s rate, four-byte frame alignment and matching
    child metadata under one random session. The guest produced digital silence
    during the 3.9-second interval, which was retained honestly rather than
    replaced with a synthetic signal. Browser and HTTP error lists were empty.
    PNG framebuffer capture operates on both production adapters and
    deterministic muted screen and sound-write assertions operate in headless
    Tests. This completes EMU-406.
- [x] EMU-407 Add performance isolation, resource budgets, background policy,
  audio latency/underrun metrics, dropped-frame state, and crash diagnostics.
  Both production adapters now publish a shared debugger contract with iframe
  and worker isolation provenance, one-session, frame, snapshot, trace, media
  and crash-history budgets, requestAnimationFrame interval statistics and
  labelled dropped-slot estimates. Hidden documents pause the genuine core and
  active audio and restore the prior run state when visible. jsbeeb reports
  AudioContext latency and measured sound-buffer gap underruns; Arculator
  reports latency and SDL queue bytes while explicitly declaring its callback
  underrun counter unavailable. Redacted browser, command and execution crash
  records retain at most 16 entries. Unit tests and deployed BBC/A310 browser
  contracts cover budgets, suspension, adapter capability and zero-error runs.

### 6.2 Machine adapters

- [ ] EMU-420 Integrate and contract-test BBC Model B adapter for first vertical
  slice, including CPU, video, sound, keyboard, DFS/media, reset, banking, state,
  and debug hooks.
- [x] EMU-421 Add second materially different 8-bit slice selected at P0 (Atom
  or Electron recommended to expose false BBC assumptions early).
  - [x] Evidence: 10 Electron adapter contracts parse the vendored ElkJS
   runtime and prove the two capability declarations cannot drift, that all 24
   unavailable capabilities carry a stated reason, and that no workbench
   command is left unclassified. The slice deliberately exposed false BBC
   assumptions: the machine profile now records cassette, Plus 1, Plus 3,
   sideways RAM and joystick as planned rather than supported, because that
   core fits none of them.
- [ ] EMU-422 Add B+ and Master profiles with explicit memory/CPU/storage tests.
  - [x] The Master 128 slice runs. A headless run on the real core with a
    locally supplied MOS 3.20 image booted the machine, called its MOS twice
    through OSWRCH, captured the exact text those calls produced, and finished
    with the expected registers and memory. That is the 65C12 CPU, the Master
    memory map and its combined firmware image all exercised together.
  - [x] The BBC B 1770 storage variant runs as a separate slice: a headless run
    with locally supplied 1770 DFS and ADFS images booted, called the MOS and
    produced the same verified result, which exercises a different filing-system
    controller from the 8271 slice.
  - [ ] The BBC B+ is blocked, not unfinished: jsbeeb 1.19.1 publishes no B+
    model, so its shadow and sideways memory behaviour cannot be executed here
    and no amount of B+ firmware would change that. A contract test asserts
    against the engine's own model list that no such model exists, so the claim
    follows the code and will fail if a future engine adds one.
- [ ] EMU-423 Add remaining Atom/Electron profiles and expansion combinations.
  - [x] The Atom tape slice runs. A headless run on the real core with locally
    supplied Atom kernel and BASIC ROMs executed 6502 code and finished with the
    expected accumulator, index registers and memory.
  - [x] Fixed a real overstatement found while proving that slice: the runner's
    `OUTPUT` and `EVENT[…]` assertions capture BBC MOS entry addresses, and on
    the Atom they were silently counting whatever occupied those addresses in a
    different operating system. Both are now refused on that machine with an
    explanation, verified by a headless run whose plan is rejected rather than
    passed. The Atom slice's own test therefore asserts only registers and
    memory, which is all it can prove.
  - [ ] The Acorn Electron is blocked, not unfinished: jsbeeb 1.19.1 publishes
    no Electron model, so its ULA, memory contention and Plus 1 or Plus 3
    expansions cannot be executed here, and supplying Electron firmware would
    not change that. The Atom MMC and DOS models exist in the engine but need
    firmware images this build registers no manifest for.
  - [ ] Candidate browser Electron cores were surveyed rather than assumed.
    `dmcoles/elkjs` is the only maintained emulator that runs an Electron in a
    browser today: GPL-2.0, about 130 KB of modular JavaScript, last changed
    November 2024. Reading its memory model settles what it can honestly
    provide: a fixed 32 KB RAM machine with exactly two ROMs, the operating
    system and BASIC, loaded from hard-coded paths. Its ROM-bank decode returns
    BASIC for every bank the keyboard does not claim, and the author's own
    commented-out code shows real sideways-ROM paging was started and not
    finished. It therefore has no Plus 1, no Plus 3, no AP5 or AP6, no ADFS, no
    cartridge and no usable expansion ROM slots. It also ships the Acorn ROMs
    and seven commercial game snapshots, none of which may be vendored here.
  - [ ] The expansions this product would want are provided by `stardot/elkulator`,
    which the sibling bit-chat tests already build and patch, and whose MAME
    expansion ROM sets are already present under `local-roms`. There is no
    upstream WebAssembly port of it, and it is built on Allegro 4, so a browser
    slice would need that port doing first, in the way `pdjstone/arculator-wasm`
    was ported to SDL2 before this build could use it for the A310.
  - [x] Decision taken: ElkJS first, then the Elkulator port. ADR 0008 records
    the adapter and the GPL position, the latter marked pending sign-off.
  - [x] The Electron now runs. Six ElkJS hardware modules are vendored at a
    pinned revision with a SHA-256 for each pristine file, no firmware and no
    game snapshot is copied, and the single local change, replacing a hard-coded
    ROM fetch with images from the browser-local vault, is recorded as a patch.
    A runtime page drives those modules directly rather than the upstream user
    interface. Its GPL-2.0 licence, provenance and patch travel with the
    container image beside jsbeeb's.
  - [x] The adapter declares twelve capabilities and twenty-four unavailable
    ones with a reason for each, because that core keeps its registers in
    closure variables reachable only through its own snapshot and runs a batch
    of cycles per call with no per-instruction hook. Every one of the
    thirty-six command types the workbench emulator panel can emit is
    classified, so a command this core cannot honour is refused in the workbench
    with the core's own recorded reason rather than sent and silently dropped.
    A contract test parses `public/electron-runtime.js` and compares its
    capability, unavailability and command tables against
    `src/emulator/electronAdapter.ts`, and separately checks that no command in
    the jsbeeb command union is left unclassified, so the two cannot drift.
  - [x] Evidence: a headed Chromium run loaded the runtime in an iframe, supplied
    the Electron operating system and BASIC ROMs from the vault path, booted the
    machine, and after 1.2 seconds of execution found the program counter inside
    the operating system ROM at &DAD4 with the stack at &FD. It then loaded a
    6502 program at &1900 and read back the real result: memory &2000 held &42,
    the accumulator &42 and X 7, with the program counter spinning in its own
    loop. Asking the adapter for a stop-address test was refused with the
    per-instruction-hook reason. Zero console errors.
  - [x] The runtime is wired into the workbench emulator panel beside the jsbeeb
    and Arculator surfaces. Selecting the Electron routes the panel to
    `/electron.html` on the `8bit-net-electron` channel rather than to jsbeeb,
    which has no Electron model and would have refused the machine after the
    session was already declared live. The adapter speaks the panel's own
    envelope, binds its session and loaded program to the runtime session
    manifest, and republishes real registers every 500 ms so the panel shows a
    live reading rather than a true-but-stale one.
  - [x] The panel does not synthesise a jsbeeb snapshot for this core. That
    shape carries video, VIA, Tube, profiler and trace state ElkJS has no way to
    observe, so the Electron reports its own state and the debugger surfaces
    that depend on the jsbeeb snapshot stay empty rather than plausible.
  - [x] Two upstream defects were found and fixed while wiring it. `memory.js`
    fetched firmware from files beside the script; the vendored copy takes ROM
    images from its caller. `processor.js` called a bare `reset6502()` from
    inside `exec6502`, which is a property of the module instance rather than a
    global and so threw a `ReferenceError` in any page that does not define that
    global, halting the machine on the first deferred reset; the reference is
    qualified to `self.reset6502()`. Both are recorded in
    `docker/elkjs/elkjs-webide.patch` and described in the provenance file.
  - [x] The Electron machine profile no longer claims expansions the running
    core does not model. Cassette, Plus 1, Plus 3, sideways RAM, joystick and
    the 1MHzPi firmware are marked planned with the Elkulator port named as the
    requirement, because enabling them changed no behaviour in the only adapter
    that can run this machine.
  - [x] Evidence for the wiring: a headless Chromium run seeded the firmware
    vault and an Electron project, reloaded the workbench, and found the panel
    routed to `/electron.html`, the machine running with its program counter
    advancing through the operating system ROM, the step control disabled
    carrying the core's per-instruction-hook reason, and the capability
    disclosure listing twelve of thirty-six capabilities offered with
    twenty-four reasons. Build and run then assembled the project and loaded it:
    the panel reported ten bytes of 6502 machine code at &1900 and the live core
    held &42 at &2000 with the accumulator &42, X 7 and the program counter
    spinning at &1907. Zero console errors.
  - [ ] Plus 1, Plus 3, AP5 and AP6 still need the Elkulator WebAssembly port;
    ElkJS models none of them and no firmware changes that.
- [ ] EMU-424 Add Tube host/parasite runtime starting with one selected second
  - [x] A BBC B with the 6502 second processor fitted boots and runs host code
    correctly: a headless run on the real core with a locally supplied Tube boot
    ROM booted the machine, called its MOS and finished with the expected
    registers and memory, so fitting a parasite does not disturb the host slice.
  - [ ] Loading and executing a program on the parasite, and asserting parasite
    registers and memory from a test plan, are not implemented; the runtime can
    read Tube memory and record transfer events but a hardware test still binds
    to the host CPU. That keeps this item open.
  processor; prove scheduling, state, media, input, and dual debugger hooks.
- [ ] EMU-425 Add other Tube CPUs only when each meets production profile gate.
- [ ] EMU-426 Integrate first ARM2/ARM3 Archimedes adapter with ROM/user flow,
  video/audio/input/storage/state/debug hooks and RISC OS application launch.
  A310/RISC OS 3.11 ROM flow, video, keyboard input, live debug, bounded HostFS
  transfer, verified Absolute application launch, live VIDC audio, framebuffer
  capture and bounded 800 KiB ADFS floppy mounting are implemented. Core-native
  save/restore, durable media write-back and the remaining lifecycle gates keep
  this parent item open.
- [ ] EMU-427 Add later Archimedes/ARM profiles based on verified equivalence.
- [x] EMU-428 Publish per-adapter limitations and accuracy/regression evidence.
  - [x] One adapter support matrix now answers, for every machine profile,
    whether this build can run it and why. It distinguishes three states that
    mean different things to a user: runnable, a model exists but no ROM
    manifest is registered here yet, and the pinned engine has no model at all
    so supplying firmware could never help. The firmware vault states the
    applicable one instead of always asking for ROMs.
  - [x] The model names the matrix claims are checked against the pinned
    engine's own published model list by contract test, so the claim cannot
    drift from the code. Nine contracts cover the states, the ROM manifests, the
    absence of an Electron or B+ model, the A310-only scope of the ARM adapter,
    and an unknown machine, which must report no support rather than invent it.

### Phase 4 exit gate

- [ ] EMU-GATE Two 8-bit slices and one scoped ARM slice can run exact resolved
  profiles with correct media/input/audio/video lifecycle; save/headless behavior
  and declared limitations pass security, accuracy, and performance tests.

## 7. Phase 5 — debugger, instruction spy, and automated tests

### 7.1 Debug protocol and session UI

Current implemented increment:

- [x] Working local debug state machine with resolved source and address
  breakpoints, continue, instruction step and reset controls.
- [x] ROM-aware BBC hardware debugger exposes live PC/A/X/Y/S/P, individual
  flags, run/pause/reset/step, execute breakpoints, memory reads and an exact
  step trace without forcing normal emulation onto the slow tracing path.
- [x] The first advanced live-debug control increment adds monotonically
  sequenced command IDs with duplicate/stale rejection, exact hook-aware
  instruction stepping, real PC instruction bytes/decoding, breakpoint
  inventory/removal, run-to, JSR step-over, hardware-stack return step-out, raw
  stack bytes and side-effect-free memory inspection. Paused edits are limited
  to 1–256 validated main-RAM bytes below &8000, emulator-acknowledged and read
  back immediately. A ROM-backed browser contract proves each path and caught
  two real hook-resume ordering defects before release.
- [x] The first structured breakpoint increment adds bounded hit targets, typed
  A/X/Y/S/P/PC comparisons, pause-with-optional-log and non-stopping logpoint
  actions, live hit counts, safe documented log placeholders, and a bounded
  64-entry event log. Unit contracts cover validation/evaluation/rendering; a
  ROM-backed browser contract proves a third-hit X=&02 conditional stop and five
  non-stopping live-register log events without fabricated state.
- [x] The first ARM2 execution-control increment publishes Arculator's complete
  installed permanent breakpoint inventory plus one clearly marked temporary
  run-to stop. The A310 debugger accepts aligned 26-bit addresses, build symbols
  and bounded offsets, supports removal/clear, continues into permanent or
  temporary stops through the real instruction hook, steps over BL by stopping
  at its following instruction, falls back to one exact step for non-BL opcodes,
  and can run to the masked live R14 return address. A ROM-backed Chromium
  contract proves permanent stop at &038021C8, temporary stop at &038021CC,
  fallback step to &038021D0 and zero browser errors. Conditions, counts and
  grouping and rebuild-aware persistence remain open in this earlier increment.
- [x] ARM2 permanent breakpoints now support a bounded 1–1,000,000 execute-hit
  threshold and unsigned live R0–R14/execute-PC comparisons (`=`, `≠`, `<`,
  `≤`, `>`, `≥`). Counts and comparisons execute inside the patched C
  instruction hook; snapshots only inspect the authoritative per-slot state.
  A ROM-backed Chromium contract proves the RISC OS memory-clear loop continues
  through its first two &038021B0 executions and stops on hit three when live PC
  equals &038021B0, then preserves removal/run-to/step behavior with no browser
  errors. ARM logpoints are now also implemented: log-only continues execution,
  pause-and-log stops in the hook, and both capture immutable R0–R14, execute-PC
  and hit count into a 64-event C-core ring with explicit overwrite accounting.
  Templates accept only documented captured-value placeholders. A raw-loop
  browser contract proves the non-stopping ring caps at 64 while running and a
  pause-and-log condition stops and records exactly on hit three. ARM permanent
  breakpoints now also accept up to four validated R0–R14/PC comparisons with
  explicit AND semantics. The C hook evaluates every predicate before acting;
  Chromium proves R0 matches on every visit while R1 permits the stop only on
  the third execution. Symbol-valued conditions and persistence were completed
  in the following project-schema increment.
- [x] DBG-505 Correct and contract-test the A310 raw-debug loader after a directly
  loaded minimal branch loop appeared to execute then escaped back into RISC OS.
  The root cause was an unmapped logical &8000 destination: all requested writes
  were silently discarded, read-back was &FF, and the old bridge still changed
  R15/refilled with unrelated bytes. The bridge now requires every destination
  page to have current MEMC backing and verifies every byte before changing CPU
  state. Because waiting for RISC OS to establish a mapping did not complete
  even within a five-minute wall-clock probe, raw debug now installs an explicit
  identity mapping only for the bounded image pages backed by installed physical
  main RAM. It then writes and verifies every byte before entering supervisor
  mode with IRQ/FIQ masked and refilling at entry+8. ROM-backed Chromium proves
  the exact 12-byte `MOV; ADD; B` image at &8000 and a third-hit live-R0 stop at
  &8004, demonstrating entry, sequential execution, branching and hook state.
- [x] First capability-scoped hardware data-watchpoint increment uses jsbeeb's
  real read/write bus hooks for one-byte read, write, or change access in mapped
  main RAM below &8000. It includes optional byte equality/inequality, exact
  triggering PC, old/new values, live access counts, removal, a 16-watchpoint
  cap and bounded 64-event history. Qualified interrupt/event/raster stops are
  implemented through their separate trace and raster hooks under DBG-504;
  banked, range and mapped-I/O data watchpoints remain explicitly unsupported.

- [x] DBG-500 Implement authenticated, sequenced, bounded, reconnectable debug
  protocol with capabilities, session ownership, backpressure, and audit. The
  production 6502 iframe transport now binds every command and response to a
  fresh random runtime-session nonce. Every toolbar and workspace command gets
  one monotonically increasing transport ID, while responses carry a separate
  monotonic event sequence; wrong-session, duplicate and stale envelopes are
  rejected after exact origin/source validation. Iframe reload resets transport
  counters, observed state and initialisation ownership before reconnect. Unit
  contracts cover session and sequence rejection. A ROM-backed Chromium flow
  proves both a wrong-session reset at ID 999999 and a correct-session stale
  reset at ID 1 leave the paused core and hook inventory unchanged, then proves
  ordinary build, debug, grouping and rebuild resolution still work with zero
  browser errors. The A310 bridge now uses the same nonce plus command/response
  sequence envelope, retaining no-token compatibility only for isolated test
  harnesses. A local-ROM fast-boot contract receives 88 strictly ordered
  responses from the one expected session, rejects wrong-session ID 999999 and
  stale correct-session ID 1 resets, then accepts pause ID 2 after RISC OS
  reaches fast-boot completion in 2.5 seconds with zero browser errors. Both
  adapters now publish protocol version, adapter identity, session ownership,
  an authoritative capability list, accepted-command count, last command ID and
  a 32-entry accepted-command audit in each snapshot. The debugger exposes this
  negotiated state in a technical disclosure. The parent permits one
  unacknowledged command and queues at most 64 more; a core releases the next
  command only with a session-bound, sequenced `command-accepted` response, and
  overflow is refused visibly. Deployed BBC proof reports session-bound jsbeeb
  protocol 2 at command 7. A310 proof reports 13 exact capabilities and an audit
  containing only accepted initialise ID 1 and pause ID 2, with both forged
  resets absent and zero browser errors.
- [x] DBG-501 Implement immutable debug session binding and state machine
  (DBG-001–DBG-003). Every Build and debug action now creates a frozen,
  versioned session record only after a successful current machine-code build.
  It binds the target identity, build fingerprint, full output SHA-256 and size,
  toolchain identity/version, copied resolved machine manifest, adapter identity
  and pinned version, exact selected ROM filename/size/SHA-256 inventory, and
  debug run profile with processor, origin, entry point, ROM-set identity and
  capabilities. The shared lifecycle distinguishes stopped, starting, running,
  paused, stepping, rewinding, terminated, crashed and disconnected. Live
  running/paused changes come from core snapshots; the initial snapshot is not
  accepted as the bound program until its PC enters the artifact range. Machine,
  profile or ROM disconnection and adapter errors have explicit terminal states.
  Stop is a real sequenced command in jsbeeb and Arculator: it cancels temporary
  execution work and pauses the core without erasing the immutable record.
  Restart can start that same binding, while a new build creates a new session.
  The debugger strip exposes concise provenance and a disclosure with full
  digests, run profile, firmware inventory and an explicit reason that unsafe
  PC-only instruction skipping is unavailable. Unit contracts cover frozen
  deep copies, provenance validation, legal lifecycle transitions and snapshot
  mapping. The ROM-backed BBC browser contract binds the exact 8-byte output
  digest plus three real MAME ROM digests, reaches Paused, accepts Stop as
  authenticated command 5, reports `debug session stopped`, retains the binding
  as Terminated and produces zero browser errors. That proof also found and
  fixed a command-ordering race: breakpoint projection now waits until the live
  PC proves the just-built artifact is loaded, and empty breakpoint collections
  have stable identities so they cannot flood the bounded command queue. The
  A310 fast-boot contract receives 89 strictly sequenced events, reaches genuine
  fast-boot completion in 2.1 seconds, then accepts pause ID 2 and Stop ID 3;
  its final snapshot reports `debug session stopped`, all three commands in the
  bounded audit and zero browser errors.
- [x] DBG-502 Complete stop, source-level step and keyboard commands. Continue,
  pause, restart, exact instruction step-in, 6502 JSR step-over, stack-return
  step-out and run-to-address are implemented with availability state. ARM2 now
  also has exact step-in, BL-aware step-over, live-R14 return and aligned run-to.
  ARM source step-in runs exact core instructions to the next different mapped
  file/line. Source step-over does the same for ordinary instructions and uses
  a real temporary instruction-hook stop at the following mapped statement for
  BL. Source maps and ranges are validated, synchronous scans yield every 256
  instructions and a bounded 100,000-instruction default prevents an unbounded
  browser stall. A ROM-backed Chromium contract proves both a one-instruction
  source step-in and a BL source step-over through a called routine to &8004.
  The 6502 adapter now also source-steps with a genuine instruction hook until
  execution reaches a different retained file/line mapping, with a bounded
  100,000-instruction failure outcome and explicit started, completed and budget
  events. Its source-over path treats JSR as a call and runs to the following
  instruction; source-out validates the live hardware stack. Both adapters now
  expose source in/over/out controls with reasons on disabled actions. The shared
  command palette provides Pause, Stop, Restart, instruction step, source in,
  source over, source out, run to cursor, run to address and run to build symbol.
  Keyboard paths are F6, Shift+F6, Ctrl+F5, Alt+F11, F11, F10, Shift+F11 and
  Ctrl+F10. Run to cursor resolves the active file and line only from the current
  immutable source map; run to symbol uses the current artifact symbol table.
  A deployed ROM-backed BBC browser flow proves F11 source in, F10 source over,
  Ctrl+F10 run to cursor, command-palette discovery and Shift+F6 Stop. The final
  protocol audit contains two source-step commands, run-to and Stop as commands
  5, 6, 7 and 9 respectively, retains all three exact ROM digests and the exact
  build digest, and reports zero browser errors.
- [x] DBG-503 Complete symbol conditions, temporary breakpoints, grouping,
  persistence and full rebuild-resolution metadata. Address/source execute
  breakpoints, typed register conditions, bounded hit counts, optional logs,
  non-stopping logpoints, live counters and removal are implemented. ARM2 has
  symbol/offset permanent breakpoints, live inventory/removal, one-shot run-to,
  bounded hit targets, unsigned R0–R14/PC comparisons, log-only and pause-and-
  log actions, a bounded authoritative event ring and safe captured-register
  templates. Up to four R0–R14/PC predicates can be combined with explicit AND
  semantics in an accessible condition editor and are evaluated authoritatively
  in the C instruction hook. Project schema 8 introduced bounded breakpoint
  intent per build target: address/symbol expression, enabled state, threshold,
  predicates, action and log template. The debugger resolves each intent anew
  against the active artifact, shows its exact output digest or an honest
  unresolved reason, and reinstalls resolved intent in the live core after a
  browser reload. Schema 9 comparison operands retain a numeric or build-symbol
  expression and resolve it against the same exact artifact as the breakpoint
  address, with explicit unresolved state after symbol removal. Unit contracts
  cover v8-to-v9 numeric migration and address/operand symbol re-resolution. A
  real A310 Chromium contract builds `_start`, `loop` and `limit`, resolves
  `limit-&8006` to &00000002, installs that comparison in Arculator at `loop`,
  and reads the same expression back from the schema-v9 project with zero
  browser errors. A separate ROM-backed Chromium contract proves project
  persistence and live-core restore across reload. Schema 10 adds bounded,
  named, build-target-scoped breakpoint groups. Group enablement projects only
  active members into Arculator without deleting intent; group deletion safely
  ungroups members. A real A310 Chromium contract creates `Rendering`, installs
  the symbolic conditional `loop` stop into that group, proves disabling the
  group empties the live-core inventory, then proves re-enabling reinstalls the
  exact numeric core condition without losing schema-v10 intent. The maintained
  ARM debugger screenshot was refreshed from this flow. Schema 11 retains the
  latest eight distinct artifact resolutions per intent, including requested
  expression, resolved ARM logical address, exact output fingerprint,
  verification state, and explicit moved, unchanged, or rejected reason. A
  native ARM rebuild Chromium contract inserts one instruction before `loop`,
  proves the symbol moves from &00008004 to &00008008 in the live core, and
  verifies both exact build fingerprints and the move reason in schema-v11
  history with zero browser errors. ARM coverage for this item is complete.
  Schema 12 adds the equivalent project-backed address and symbol intent for
  6502 and 65C12 targets, with typed symbolic register conditions, hit counts,
  pause or log action, named enableable groups, and eight exact output-digest
  resolution records. The browser projects enabled intent and existing source
  gutter stops into one deduplicated set, then replaces the real jsbeeb
  instruction hooks atomically. Unit contracts cover symbol and condition
  resolution, disabled groups, unresolved symbols, migration, rebuild movement
  and duplicate-proof suppression. A ROM-backed Chromium contract installs
  symbolic `loop` at &1902 beside an independent &1907 source stop, proves
  disabling `Gameplay` removes only `loop`, then restores the group. It inserts
  `NOP`, rebuilds, verifies the real jsbeeb hook moved to &1903, and reads the
  two exact SHA-256 fingerprints plus `moved from &1902 to &1903` from schema
  12 with zero browser errors. The maintained 6502 debugger screenshot was
  refreshed from the expanded resolution history in this flow.
- [x] DBG-504 Implement data, interrupt, event, raster breakpoints capability-
  gated by adapter; never emulate unsupported semantics deceptively. Qualified
  jsbeeb targets provide one-byte mapped-main-RAM read/write/change stops through
  real bus hooks, plus instruction-address, opcode, exact memory-access and
  IRQ/NMI-transition event triggers through the independent trace hook. Raster
  stops observe the selected core's real frame, sync, mode, palette and beam
  counters at instruction boundaries and pause on a validated event or exact
  supported position. All rings, post-trigger windows and hook counts are
  bounded. These controls are exposed only by the ROM-backed adapter. Banked
  ranges, mapped I/O, device-specific events and adapters without the required
  hooks remain visibly unsupported rather than being inferred.

### 7.2 State and navigation

  - [x] Evidence: 3 breakpoint-model contracts and 3 watchpoint contracts
   covering address, opcode and exact memory-access stops, with 3 6502
   persistence contracts and 5 ARM persistence contracts proving intents
   survive a project round trip. Capability gating is proved by the 10
   Electron adapter contracts, which parse the vendored runtime and assert
   that every command the workbench can send is classified, so an unsupported
   semantic is refused rather than imitated.
- [x] Live 6502 register/flag, PC-relative memory, program output and bounded
  source-linked instruction trace views for the diagnostic adapter.
- [x] First ROM-aware memory inspection reads a bounded 64-byte range from the
  live machine; browser verification confirms the BASIC program at PAGE.
- [x] Paused hardware register editing now writes A/X/Y/S/P/PC as one bounded,
  sequenced emulator transaction with width validation, core-authoritative
  read-back, before/after history, P interrupt-state recalculation and safe PC
  hook-resume invalidation. Running sessions reject edits; the browser contract
  proves the next real instruction consumes edited PC and register values.
- [x] The first authoritative ARM2 state-panel increment separates the packed
  26-bit R15 address from N/Z/C/V/I/F and User/FIQ/IRQ/Supervisor mode, exposes
  Arculator's actual execute/decode instruction latches, labels a separately
  side-effect-free next-fetch preview, and reads R8–R14 from all four real core
  register banks with the selected bank marked. The pinned core patch compiles
  from its immutable revision; a ROM-backed Chromium contract proves decoded
  pipeline state and bank selection.
- [x] Paused ARM2 register editing now writes full-width R0–R14 values through a
  bounded core ABI and accepts R15 only as an aligned 26-bit execute address.
  R15 edits preserve flags, interrupt masks and mode, convert the visible
  execute address to stored PC+8 and refill the real pipeline. The editor retains
  a pending value until a later authoritative snapshot matches it rather than
  trusting a transient acknowledgement. ROM-backed Chromium proves R2 changes
  from &039FFFD8 to &12345678 and is restored exactly while the raw loop is
  paused. It also proves an execute-PC move from &00008004 to &00008008,
  authoritative pipeline refill, preservation of the packed status/mode bits,
  and exact PC restoration. Running sessions reject writes; the combined
  live-core contract completes with zero browser errors.
- [x] The first A310 hardware-inspector increment reads VIDC display/frame/
  clock/DMA/video/cursor state, MEMC revision/refresh/video/cursor/sound DMA and
  sound-window state, and IOC A/B/F status/masks plus four timer counter/latches
  directly from Arculator internals. Mapped I/O is never polled. IOC sources are
  decoded as asserted, enabled or genuinely pending (`status & mask`) rather
  than conflating those states. The WASM compiler rejected an unlinked upstream
  framebuffer declaration; it was removed, with dimensions explicitly sourced
  from the live SDL canvas. A ROM-backed Chromium contract proves live values
  and instruction stepping with zero browser errors.

- [x] DBG-520 Implement 6502/65C02 register, flag, interrupt, opcode, effective-
  address, and cycle panels (DBG-020–DBG-021). The live adapter now identifies

  source mask/line/acceptance plus NMI line/edge, decodes the core-specific
  opcode table, resolves all represented 6502-family addressing modes with
  pointer/branch/page-cross detail, and records exact step-in cycle/register/
  flag/interrupt deltas. ROM-backed contracts prove Model B indexed-address

  DBG-540–DBG-542.
- [x] DBG-521 Implement ARM register/mode/banked-state/pipeline/coprocessor panel
  for actual selected cores (DBG-022). The qualified A310 ARM2 adapter exposes

  sourced execute, decode and next-fetch pipeline stages. Its pinned machine
  profile configures `fpa = 0`; snapshots now publish that authoritative absence,

- [x] DBG-522 Implement Tube host/parasite side-by-side state and focus (DBG-023).
  The qualified 6502 Tube session now presents authoritative host and parasite

  - [x] Evidence: 3 debug-protocol contracts and 4 debug-session contracts

- [x] DBG-523 Complete decoded call stack, globals, locals, parameters, watches

- [x] DBG-524 Complete bank/address-space selection, paging, symbols, formats,

### 7.3 Instruction spy and hardware inspection

  address, register/flag deltas, IRQ/NMI transitions, up to 24 actual data/I/O

  versioned JSON/text export are functional. The UI reports high timing impact,
  overwritten records/access drops and live/display windows. A ROM-backed
  contract proves 21 instructions, five source-linked STX records, the real
  &2000 write, exports/bookmark and an &8E pause at PC &1906.

  five-record `2 pre + trigger + 2 post` PC window and pause at &1902.
